  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cyhyraeth adalah roh hantu dalam mitologi Welsh, suara rintihan tanpa wujud yang terdengar sebelum seseorang meninggal.",
    "en": "The cyhyraeth is a ghostly spirit of Welsh myth, a bodiless groaning voice heard ahead of someone's death.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cyhyraeth-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Cyhyraeth adalah roh hantu dalam mitologi Welsh: suara rintihan tanpa tubuh yang terdengar menjelang kematian seseorang. Legenda mengaitkannya dengan daerah sekitar Sungai Tywi di Dyfed timur dan pesisir Glamorganshire. Suaranya mirip erangan orang sakit parah dan terdengar tiga kali, makin lemah setiap kali, sebagai peringatan sebelum orang itu wafat. Di pesisir Glamorganshire ia terdengar sebelum kapal karam, disertai cahaya mayat (corpse-light).",
      "en": "The cyhyraeth is a ghostly spirit of Welsh mythology, a voice without a body that moans as someone nears death. Legend places it around the River Tywi in eastern Dyfed and along the Glamorganshire coast. It sounds like the groans of a gravely ill person and is heard three times, each fainter than the last, as a warning before the death. On the Glamorganshire coast it is heard ahead of a shipwreck, together with a corpse-light.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cyhyraeth-c01",
        "cyhyraeth-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seperti banshee Irlandia dan Cailleach Skotlandia, yang berkerabat dekat dengan cyhyraeth dan Gwrach y Rhibyn, cyhyraeth juga meratap bagi orang Wales yang meninggal jauh dari kampung halaman. Kisahnya kadang dicampuradukkan dengan Gwrach y Rhibyn (Hag of the Mist). Asal namanya tidak jelas: unsur pertama mungkin dari kata Welsh cyhyr (otot, urat, daging) atau dari oer (dingin), dan unsur akhir mungkin kata benda aeth (sakit, duka, takut).",
      "en": "Much like the Irish banshee and the Scottish Cailleach, both close kin to the cyhyraeth and the Gwrach y Rhibyn, it also laments Welsh people who die far from home. Its legend is at times blended with that of the Gwrach y Rhibyn (Hag of the Mist). The name's origin is uncertain: the first part may come from Welsh cyhyr, 'muscle, sinew, flesh', or from oer, 'cold', and the ending may be the noun aeth, 'pain, grief, fear'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cyhyraeth-c03",
        "cyhyraeth-c04",
        "cyhyraeth-c05",
        "cyhyraeth-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "cyhyraeth-c01",
      "source_id": "cyhyraeth-s1",
      "quote": "is a ghostly spirit in Welsh mythology, a disembodied moaning voice that sounds before a person's death. Legends associate the cyhyraeth with the area around the River Tywi in eastern Dyfed, as well as the coast of Glamorganshire.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh hantu mitologi Welsh (Wales), suara rintihan sebelum kematian; dikaitkan dengan Sungai Tywi (River Tywi), Dyfed timur, dan pesisir Glamorganshire.",
        "en": "A ghostly spirit of Welsh mythology (Wales), a moaning voice before death; linked to the River Tywi, eastern Dyfed and the Glamorganshire coast."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cyhyraeth-c02",
      "source_id": "cyhyraeth-s1",
      "quote": "to sound three times (growing weaker and fainter each time) as a threefold warning before the person expires. Along the Glamorganshire coast, the cyhyraeth is said to be heard before a shipwreck, accompanied by a corpse-light.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terdengar tiga kali sebagai peringatan; di pesisir Glamorganshire sebelum kapal karam, dengan cahaya mayat (corpse-light).",
        "en": "Sounds three times as a warning; on the Glamorganshire coast before a shipwreck, with a corpse-light."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cyhyraeth-c03",
      "source_id": "cyhyraeth-s1",
      "quote": "Like the Irish banshee and the Scottish Cailleach, to which the cyhyraeth and the Gwrach y Rhibyn are closely related, the cyhyraeth also sounds for Welsh natives dying far from home.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seperti banshee Irlandia dan Cailleach Skotlandia, berkerabat dengan Gwrach y Rhibyn; meratap bagi orang Wales yang mati di perantauan.",
        "en": "Like the Irish banshee and Scottish Cailleach, related to the Gwrach y Rhibyn; sounds for Welsh natives dying far from home."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cyhyraeth-c04",
      "source_id": "cyhyraeth-s1",
      "quote": "An alternative possibility is that cyhyr is from cyoer, from oer \"cold\", with the last element being the noun aeth meaning \"pain\", \"woe\", \"grief\", \"fear\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asal nama: cyhyr mungkin dari oer (dingin); aeth berarti sakit, duka, takut.",
        "en": "Etymology: cyhyr possibly from oer 'cold'; aeth meaning pain, woe, grief, fear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cyhyraeth-c05",
      "source_id": "cyhyraeth-s1",
      "quote": "The legend of the cyhyraeth is sometimes conflated with tales of the Gwrach-y-Rhibyn",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah cyhyraeth kadang dicampur dengan Gwrach y Rhibyn (Hag of the Mist).",
        "en": "The cyhyraeth legend is sometimes conflated with the Gwrach y Rhibyn (Hag of the Mist)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cyhyraeth-c06",
      "source_id": "cyhyraeth-s2",
      "quote": "sind Geister der walisischen Mythologie mit einer körperlosen Stimme, die vor dem Tod eines Menschen zu hören sind.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh mitologi Welsh dengan suara tanpa tubuh sebelum kematian.",
        "en": "Spirits of Welsh mythology with a bodiless voice heard before a death."

    },
    {
      "id": "cyhyraeth-c07",
      "source_id": "cyhyraeth-s2",
      "quote": "Wie die schottischen Cailleach und die irischen Banshee klagen Gwrach und Cyhyraeth auch über den Tod von Landsleuten in der Fremde.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fenodyree adalah makhluk gaib berbulu dalam folklor Pulau Man, sejenis peri yang membantu pekerjaan manusia seperti brownie.",
    "en": "The fenodyree is a hairy supernatural being of Isle of Man folklore, a fairy that helps people with their chores, much like a brownie.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fenodyree-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Pulau Man (Isle of Man), fenodyree adalah makhluk gaib berbulu, sejenis peri (Manx: ferrishyn), yang kerap mengerjakan pekerjaan berat untuk manusia, mirip brownie di Skotlandia dan Inggris. Ia mengangkut balok batu besar dan memotong rumput padang dengan kecepatan luar biasa, sehingga dijuluki yn foldyr gastey, \"si pemotong rumput yang gesit\". Hadiah pakaian justru membuatnya pergi.",
      "en": "In the folklore of the Isle of Man the fenodyree is a hairy supernatural being, a kind of fairy (Manx ferrishyn), that often does heavy work for people, comparable to the brownies of Scotland and England. It hauls huge blocks of stone and cuts meadow grass astonishingly fast, earning the name yn foldyr gastey, 'the nimble mower'. A present of clothes, however, makes it leave.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fenodyree-c01",
        "fenodyree-c03",
        "fenodyree-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Joseph Train dan J. F. Campbell mengartikan namanya \"yang berbulu\". Ada penulis yang menyebutnya raksasa yang lebih kuat dari manusia, tetapi I. H. Leney menyebut tingginya dua kaki. Dalam satu kisah ia dulunya ksatria Istana Peri yang diubah menjadi berwujud mirip satir sebagai hukuman karena jatuh cinta pada gadis manusia dari Glen Aldyn dan melewatkan pesta panen. Kisah terkenalnya, \"The Fynoderee of Gordon\", diterbitkan Sophia Morrison pada 1911. Artikel de mencatat cerita bahwa ia pernah mencoba mengangkut air dengan saringan.",
      "en": "Joseph Train and later J. F. Campbell glossed the name as 'the hairy one'. Some authors call it a giant stronger than any man, while I. H. Leney gives its height as two feet. One tale makes it a former knight of the Fairy Court, changed into a satyr-like shape as a penalty for courting a mortal girl of Glen Aldyn and missing the harvest festivities. Its best-known story, 'The Fynoderee of Gordon', was printed by Sophia Morrison in 1911. The de article adds a tale of it trying to carry water in a sieve.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fenodyree-c02",
        "fenodyree-c05",
        "fenodyree-c06",
        "fenodyree-c07",
        "fenodyree-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "fenodyree-c01",
      "source_id": "fenodyree-s1",
      "quote": "in the folklore of the Isle of Man, is a hairy supernatural creature, a sort of sprite or fairy (Manx: ferrishyn ), often carrying out chores to help humans, like the brownies of the larger areas of Scotland and England.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk gaib berbulu folklor Pulau Man (Isle of Man), sejenis peri (Manx: ferrishyn), membantu manusia seperti brownie di Skotlandia (Scotland) dan Inggris (England).",
        "en": "A hairy supernatural being of Isle of Man folklore, a fairy (Manx ferrishyn) helping humans like brownies of Scotland and England."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenodyree-c02",
      "source_id": "fenodyree-s1",
      "quote": "Fenodyree has also been glossed simply as \"the hairy one\" or \"something hairy\" in Manx by Joseph Train and J. F. Campbell after him.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Joseph Train dan J. F. Campbell mengartikan nama itu \"yang berbulu\".",
        "en": "Joseph Train and J. F. Campbell glossed the name 'the hairy one'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenodyree-c03",
      "source_id": "fenodyree-s1",
      "quote": "performing arduous tasks such as transporting great blocks of stone, or clipping meadow grass with stupendous speed. For his talent in the grass-cutting skill, he has earned the nickname yn foldyr gastey or \"the nimble mower\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mengangkut balok batu dan memotong rumput sangat cepat; dijuluki yn foldyr gastey.",
        "en": "Carries stone blocks and mows grass at great speed; nicknamed yn foldyr gastey."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenodyree-c04",
      "source_id": "fenodyree-s1",
      "quote": "On the other hand, a gift of clothing would drive the creature away, as attested in several tales.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hadiah pakaian mengusirnya.",
        "en": "A gift of clothing drives it away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenodyree-c05",
      "source_id": "fenodyree-s1",
      "quote": "Some writers suggest it was a \"giant\", or \"big and shaggy, with fiery eyes, and stronger than any man\", but I. H. Leney (Mrs. J. W. Russell) explains it measured two feet in height",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian penulis menyebutnya raksasa yang lebih kuat dari manusia; I. H. Leney menyebut tingginya dua kaki.",
        "en": "Some writers call it a giant stronger than any man; I. H. Leney says it was two feet tall."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenodyree-c06",
      "source_id": "fenodyree-s1",
      "quote": "He was transformed into a grotesque satyr-like appearance as punishment, after falling in love with a human girl from Glen Aldyn and skipping attendance of the royal high festivities of the harvest",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mantan ksatria Istana Peri (Knight of the Fairy Court) dihukum menjadi mirip satir karena mencintai gadis Glen Aldyn dan melewatkan pesta panen.",
        "en": "A former Knight of the Fairy Court punished with a satyr-like form for loving a Glen Aldyn girl and missing the harvest festival."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenodyree-c07",
      "source_id": "fenodyree-s1",
      "quote": "A version that collates several of the fairy's labor is \"The Fynoderee of Gordon\", published in 1911 by Sophia Morrison.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "The Fynoderee of Gordon diterbitkan Sophia Morrison pada 1911.",
        "en": "The Fynoderee of Gordon was published by Sophia Morrison in 1911."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenodyree-c08",
      "source_id": "fenodyree-s2",
      "quote": "Alternative Namen und Schreibweisen sind Fendodyree, Phynodderee, Phynnodderee, Fynnoderee oder sogar Yn Foldyr Gastey, was so viel heißt wie „der flinke Mäher“.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama lain: Phynodderee, Phynnodderee, Fynnoderee, Yn Foldyr Gastey (si pemotong rumput gesit).",
        "en": "Other names: Phynodderee, Phynnodderee, Fynnoderee, Yn Foldyr Gastey ('the nimble mower')."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenodyree-c09",
      "source_id": "fenodyree-s2",
      "quote": "Dieser Mangel zeigt sich in der Sage, dass einer von ihnen einmal versucht haben soll, Wasser in einem Sieb zu transportieren.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel de (Sage), seorang fenodyree pernah mencoba mengangkut air dengan saringan.",
        "en": "Per the de article (Sage), one tried to carry water in a sieve."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Tidak ada gambar dengan lisensi yang diperiksa."
    },
    {
      "field": "sources",

    },

    }
  ]
}

    "id": "Grimalkin adalah kucing peri dalam legenda Skotlandia yang tinggal di dataran tinggi; nama ini juga istilah kuno untuk kucing.",
    "en": "In Scottish legend the grimalkin is a fairy cat living in the Highlands; the word is also an old term for a cat.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Grimalkin, juga greymalkin, adalah istilah kuno untuk kucing, dari kata \"grey\" (kelabu) ditambah \"malkin\", kata kuno yang berasal dari bentuk panggilan sayang untuk nama perempuan Maud. Legenda Skotlandia menyebut grimalkin sebagai kucing peri yang tinggal di dataran tinggi (Highlands). Artikel fy menambahkan bahwa kisah rakyat tentang kucing semacam ini setidaknya berasal dari Abad Pertengahan.",
      "en": "Grimalkin, or greymalkin, is an old word for a cat, built from 'grey' and 'malkin', an archaic word derived from a pet form of the woman's name Maud. Scottish legend treats the grimalkin as a fairy cat of the Highlands. The fy article adds that folk stories about such cats go back at least to the Middle Ages.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "grimalkin-c01",

      "id": "Pada masa modern awal, nama grimalkin, dan kucing pada umumnya, dikaitkan dengan iblis dan sihir; perempuan yang diadili sebagai penyihir pada abad ke-16 hingga ke-18 sering dituduh memiliki familiar, kerap seekor grimalkin. Dalam Macbeth karya Shakespeare, Babak 1 Adegan 1, Penyihir Pertama berseru \"I come, Graymalkin\" kepada familiarnya.",
      "en": "In the early modern era the name, and cats generally, came to be tied to the devil and witchcraft; women tried as witches between the 16th and 18th centuries were often charged with keeping a familiar, frequently a grimalkin.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "grimalkin-c01",
      "source_id": "grimalkin-s1",
      "quote": "A grimalkin, also known as a greymalkin, is an archaic term for a cat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Grimalkin atau greymalkin, istilah kuno untuk kucing.",
        "en": "Grimalkin or greymalkin, an archaic term for a cat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grimalkin-c02",
      "source_id": "grimalkin-s1",
      "quote": "Scottish legend makes reference to the grimalkin as a faery cat that dwells in the highlands.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Skotlandia (Scottish, Skotlandia): kucing peri yang tinggal di dataran tinggi (Highlands).",
        "en": "Scottish legend: a fairy cat dwelling in the Highlands."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grimalkin-c03",
      "source_id": "grimalkin-s1",
      "quote": "During the early modern period, the name grimalkin – and cats in general – became associated with the devil and witchcraft. Women tried as witches in the 16th, 17th and 18th centuries were often accused of having a familiar, frequently a grimalkin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dikaitkan dengan iblis dan sihir; perempuan terdakwa penyihir abad ke-16, 17, 18 dituduh memiliki familiar grimalkin.",
        "en": "Associated with the devil and witchcraft; women tried as witches in the 16th, 17th, 18th centuries accused of a grimalkin familiar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grimalkin-c04",
      "source_id": "grimalkin-s1",
      "quote": "In Macbeth, Act 1, Scene 1, the First Witch says, \"I come, Graymalkin\" to her familiar (Shakespeare 1.1.8).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Macbeth (Shakespeare), Babak (Act) 1 Adegan (Scene) 1, Penyihir Pertama (First Witch): I come, Graymalkin.",
        "en": "In Shakespeare's Macbeth, Act 1, Scene 1, the First Witch: I come, Graymalkin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grimalkin-c05",
      "source_id": "grimalkin-s2",
      "quote": "Yn 'e folkloare fan Skotlân wurdt de term grimalkin spesifyk brûkt foar in type mytysk wêzen yn 'e foarm fan in kat. Sokke grimalkins soene yn 'e Skotske Heechlannen libje. Folksferhalen oer sokke katten geane teminsten tebek oant yn 'e Midsiuwen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam folklor Skotlandia (Skotlân), makhluk mitos berwujud kucing di Highlands (Heechlannen); kisahnya setidaknya dari Abad Pertengahan (Midsiuwen, Middle Ages).",
        "en": "In Scottish folklore (Skotlân), a mythical cat-shaped being of the Highlands (Heechlannen); tales date at least to the Middle Ages (Midsiuwen)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grimalkin-c06",
      "source_id": "grimalkin-s1",
      "quote": "The term stems from \"grey\" (the colour) plus \"malkin\", an archaic term with several meanings (a low class woman, a weakling, a mop, or a name) derived from a hypocoristic form of the female name Maud.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dari grey (kelabu) + malkin, dari bentuk panggilan nama perempuan Maud.",
        "en": "From grey + malkin, from a pet form of the female name Maud."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grimalkin-c07",
      "source_id": "grimalkin-s2",
      "quote": "Yn 1606 naam de Ingelske toanielskriuwer William Shakespeare yn syn trageedzje Macbeth",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1606 William Shakespeare memasukkan kucing Grimalkin dalam tragedi Macbeth.",
        "en": "In 1606 William Shakespeare included the cat Grimalkin in his tragedy Macbeth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grimalkin-c08",
      "source_id": "grimalkin-s1",
      "quote": "In Roger Zelazny's fantasy novel A Night in the Lonesome October (1993), \"Graymalk\" is the name of a cat owned by a witch, possibly in reference to Macbeth.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Novel Roger Zelazny A Night in the Lonesome October (1993): kucing penyihir bernama Graymalk.",
        "en": "Roger Zelazny's novel A Night in the Lonesome October (1993): a witch's cat named Graymalk."
      }
    }

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Habetrot adalah tokoh folklor daerah perbatasan Inggris Utara dan Dataran Rendah Skotlandia yang dikaitkan dengan pemintalan.",
    "en": "Habetrot is a folklore figure of the Border counties of northern England and Lowland Scotland, linked with spinning.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "habetrot-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Habetrot (juga Habitrot, Habtrot, Habbitrot) adalah tokoh folklor wilayah perbatasan (Border) Inggris Utara dan Dataran Rendah Skotlandia, yang dikaitkan dengan pemintalan dan roda pintal. Ia muncul dalam dongeng dari Selkirkshire, varian tipe ATU 501 (\"The Three Old Spinning Women\"): seorang perempuan tua cacat yang tinggal di bawah tanah bersama para pemintal lain yang tubuhnya rusak akibat pekerjaan itu; satu-satunya pemintal lain yang bernama adalah Scantlie Mab.",
      "en": "Habetrot (also Habitrot, Habtrot, Habbitrot) belongs to the folklore of the Border counties of northern England and Lowland Scotland and is tied to spinning and the spinning wheel. She appears in a Selkirkshire tale of type ATU 501 ('The Three Old Spinning Women') as a misshapen old woman dwelling underground among fellow spinners whose bodies the work has deformed; the only other spinner given a name is Scantlie Mab.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "habetrot-c01",
        "habetrot-c02",
        "habetrot-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Habetrot memintal benang untuk seorang gadis setempat, lalu meyakinkan suami baru gadis itu agar istrinya tidak pernah memintal lagi. Kisah serupa ada di Jerman (The Three Spinners) dan Norwegia (The Three Aunts). Dalam folklor perbatasan, orang yang menginjak makam bayi yang belum dibaptis terkena penyakit \"grave-merels\"; obatnya hanyalah memakai karung linen yang antara lain dipintal oleh Habetrot.",
      "en": "She spun yarn for a girl of the district and then persuaded the girl's new husband that his wife must never spin again. Comparable stories exist in Germany (The Three Spinners) and Norway (The Three Aunts). Border lore held that treading on the graves of unbaptised infants brought on 'grave-merels', curable only by wearing a linen sack that, among other conditions, had been spun by Habetrot.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "habetrot-c03",
        "habetrot-c04",
        "habetrot-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "habetrot-c01",
      "source_id": "habetrot-s1",
      "quote": "Habetrot (Habitrot, Habtrot and Habbitrot) is a figure in folklore of the Border counties of Northern England and Lowland Scotland, associated with spinning and the spinning wheel.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Habetrot (Habitrot, Habtrot, Habbitrot), tokoh folklor daerah perbatasan (Border) Inggris Utara (Northern England) dan Dataran Rendah Skotlandia (Lowland Scotland), terkait pemintalan.",
        "en": "Habetrot (Habitrot, Habtrot, Habbitrot), a folklore figure of the Border counties of Northern England and Lowland Scotland, linked with spinning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "habetrot-c02",
      "source_id": "habetrot-s1",
      "quote": "Habetrot appears in a Selkirkshire folktale which is a variant of the Aarne–Thompson–Uther Index tale type ATU 501, \"The Three Old Spinning Women\". She is an old, deformed woman who lives underground with a group of other spinsters",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dongeng Selkirkshire, tipe ATU 501 (The Three Old Spinning Women); perempuan tua cacat yang tinggal di bawah tanah bersama pemintal lain.",
        "en": "A Selkirkshire tale of type ATU 501 (The Three Old Spinning Women); an old deformed woman living underground with other spinners."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "habetrot-c03",
      "source_id": "habetrot-s1",
      "quote": "The only other named spinster is Scantlie Mab. Habetrot spun yarn for a local girl and then convinced the girl's new husband that she should never spin again.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Scantlie Mab; Habetrot memintal untuk seorang gadis lalu meyakinkan suaminya agar ia tak memintal lagi.",
        "en": "Scantlie Mab; Habetrot spun for a girl and convinced her husband she should never spin again."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "habetrot-c04",
      "source_id": "habetrot-s1",
      "quote": "Similar tales appear in countries such as Germany (such as The Three Spinners) and Norway (The Three Aunts).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah serupa di Jerman (Germany, The Three Spinners) dan Norwegia (Norway, The Three Aunts).",
        "en": "Similar tales in Germany (The Three Spinners) and Norway (The Three Aunts)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "habetrot-c05",
      "source_id": "habetrot-s2",
      "quote": "L'unica altra filatrice nominata è Scantlie Mab. Habetrot filava il filato per una ragazza del posto e poi convinse il nuovo marito della ragazza che non avrebbe mai più dovuto filare.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel it: Scantlie Mab; Habetrot memintal untuk gadis setempat (Habetrot filava).",
        "en": "it article: Scantlie Mab; Habetrot spun for a local girl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "habetrot-c06",
      "source_id": "habetrot-s1",
      "quote": "The only way to relieve this was for the afflicted to wear a sack made from linen grown in a field using manure from a farmyard that has not been disturbed for forty years, spun by Habetrot",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penyakit grave-merels dari makam bayi tak dibaptis hanya reda dengan karung linen yang dipintal Habetrot (Border).",
        "en": "Grave-merels from unchristened graves is relieved only by a linen sack spun by Habetrot (Border lore)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Katie King adalah nama yang diberikan kaum Spiritualis tahun 1870-an kepada sosok yang mereka yakini sebagai roh yang mewujud (materialisasi) dalam seans.",
    "en": "Katie King is the name 1870s Spiritualists gave to what they took to be a spirit materialising at séances.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "katie-king-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Katie King adalah nama yang diberikan kaum Spiritualis pada 1870-an kepada apa yang mereka percayai sebagai roh yang mewujud. Apakah roh itu nyata atau tipuan menjadi kontroversi publik pada pertengahan 1870-an. Ia dikatakan pertama muncul antara 1871 dan 1874 dalam seans medium Florence Cook di London, kemudian pada 1874–1875 di New York dalam seans pasangan medium Jennie dan Nelson Holmes. Kaum Spiritualis menganggapnya putri John King, roh pengendali (spirit control) yang mengaku roh bajak laut Henry Morgan.",
      "en": "Katie King was the name 1870s Spiritualists gave to what they held to be a materialised spirit, and whether she was genuine or a fraud became a public dispute in the middle of that decade. She reportedly first appeared from 1871 to 1874 at séances given by Florence Cook in London, and in 1874–1875 in New York with the mediums Jennie and Nelson Holmes. Spiritualists regarded her as the daughter of John King, a spirit control claiming to be the buccaneer Henry Morgan.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "katie-king-c01",
        "katie-king-c02",
        "katie-king-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada 9 Desember 1873 di Hackney, pengacara William Volckman menangkap tangan dan pinggang \"roh\" itu dan menuduhnya Cook yang menyamar. Ilmuwan Sir William Crookes kemudian menelitinya antara 1871 dan 1874; artikel es menyebut ia memperoleh 44 foto. Di New York, seorang perempuan bernama Eliza White mengaku pernah menyamar sebagai Katie, dan wajahnya cocok dengan foto-foto yang dijual keluarga Holmes.",
      "en": "At Hackney on 9 December 1873 the lawyer William Volckman grabbed the 'spirit' by the hand and waist and accused it of being Cook in disguise. The scientist Sir William Crookes later studied the phenomenon between 1871 and 1874; the es article credits him with 44 photographs. In New York a woman named Eliza White said she had posed as Katie, and her face matched the photographs the Holmeses sold.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "katie-king-c03",
        "katie-king-c04",
        "katie-king-c05",
        "katie-king-c07"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Raja Goldemar adalah kurcaci atau kobold dari mitologi dan folklor Jermanik, yang pada Abad Pertengahan dianggap raja para kurcaci.",
    "en": "King Goldemar is a dwarf or kobold of Germanic myth and folklore, regarded by the Middle Ages as king of the dwarfs.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "king-goldemar-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Raja Goldemar (juga Goldmar, Vollmar, Volmar) adalah kurcaci atau kobold dari mitologi dan folklor Jermanik; pada Abad Pertengahan ia telah menjadi raja kurcaci dalam kepercayaan Jerman. Penggalan puisi epik Goldemar karya Albrecht von Kemenaten mengisahkan pertemuan Dietrich dengan raja kurcaci itu, dan ia juga muncul dalam \"Der junge König und die Schäferin\" karya Ludwig Uhland. Saudara-saudaranya, Alberich (Elberich) dan Elbegast, tampil dalam puisi lain.",
      "en": "King Goldemar (also Goldmar, Vollmar, Volmar) is a dwarf or kobold of Germanic myth and folklore who by the Middle Ages was held in German belief to rule the dwarfs. Surviving fragments of Albrecht von Kemenaten's epic Goldemar describe Dietrich meeting the dwarf king, and he also figures in Ludwig Uhland's 'Der junge König und die Schäferin'. His brothers Alberich (Elberich) and Elbegast appear in other poems.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "king-goldemar-c01",
        "king-goldemar-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda yang dicatat Thomas Keightley pada 1850, Goldemar adalah kobold, roh rumah, yang tinggal bersama Neveling von Hardenberg di Kastel Hardenstein di tepi Sungai Ruhr. Ia pandai bermain harpa, gemar berjudi dadu, membawa keberuntungan, dan tak mau dilihat meski boleh diraba. Setelah tiga tahun, seorang yang penasaran menaburkan abu untuk melihat jejaknya; Goldemar mencincang orang itu, memasak dan memakannya, lalu pergi sambil meninggalkan pesan bahwa rumah itu akan sial sebagaimana dulu beruntung.",
      "en": "In a legend Thomas Keightley recorded in 1850, Goldemar was a kobold, a household spirit, living with Neveling von Hardenberg at Castle Hardenstein by the River Ruhr. He played the harp well, liked dice, brought luck to the house, and would be felt but never seen. After three years a curious man scattered ashes to find his footprints; Goldemar hacked him apart, cooked and ate him, and departed, leaving word that the house would now be as unlucky as it had been lucky.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "king-goldemar-c03",
        "king-goldemar-c04",
        "king-goldemar-c05",
        "king-goldemar-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {

      "url": "https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goldemar",
      "title": "Goldemar",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (it)",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "king-goldemar-c01",
      "source_id": "king-goldemar-s1",
      "quote": "is a dwarf or kobold from Germanic mythology and folklore. By the Middle Ages, Goldemar had become the king of the dwarfs in German belief.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Raja Goldemar (Goldmar, Vollmar, Volmar), kurcaci/kobold Jermanik; raja kurcaci Abad Pertengahan (Middle Ages) dalam kepercayaan Jerman (German); saudaranya Alberich (Elberich) dan Elbegast; Der junge König und die Schäferin karya Ludwig Uhland.",
        "en": "King Goldemar (Goldmar, Vollmar, Volmar), Germanic dwarf/kobold; king of the dwarfs by the Middle Ages in German belief; brothers Alberich (Elberich) and Elbegast; Ludwig Uhland's Der junge König und die Schäferin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "king-goldemar-c02",
      "source_id": "king-goldemar-s1",
      "quote": "Fragments of an epic poem by Albrecht von Kemenaten called Goldemar survive. The poem tells of Dietrich's encounter with the dwarf king.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggalan puisi epik Goldemar karya Albrecht von Kemenaten: pertemuan Dietrich dengan raja kurcaci.",
        "en": "Fragments of Albrecht von Kemenaten's epic Goldemar: Dietrich meets the dwarf king."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "king-goldemar-c03",
      "source_id": "king-goldemar-s1",
      "quote": "Goldemar lived with Neveling von Hardenberg at Castle Hardenstein at the Ruhr River. Goldemar frequently interacted with mortals. He called Neveling his \"brother-in-law\" and often slept in the same bed with him. He skillfully played the harp",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Thomas Keightley (1850), kobold yang tinggal bersama Neveling von Hardenberg di Kastel (Castle) Hardenstein, Sungai (River) Ruhr; pandai bermain harpa.",
        "en": "Per Thomas Keightley (1850), a kobold living with Neveling von Hardenberg at Castle Hardenstein on the Ruhr River; skilled harpist."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "king-goldemar-c04",
      "source_id": "king-goldemar-s1",
      "quote": "The spirit refused to be seen, but he would allow mortals to feel him; Keightley says that \"[h]is hands were thin like those of a frog, cold and soft to the feel.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menolak dilihat tetapi boleh diraba; tangannya seperti katak (Keightley).",
        "en": "Refused to be seen but could be felt; hands like a frog's (Keightley)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "king-goldemar-c05",
      "source_id": "king-goldemar-s1",
      "quote": "Goldemar cut the man to pieces, put them on the fire to roast, and put the head and legs in a pot to boil. He then took the cooked meat to his chambers and ate it with glee. The next day, Goldemar was gone.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Goldemar mencincang, memasak dan memakan orang yang menabur abu, lalu pergi; rumah akan sial.",
        "en": "Goldemar cut up, cooked and ate the man who strewed ashes, then left; the house would be unlucky."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "king-goldemar-c06",
      "source_id": "king-goldemar-s2",
      "quote": "Viveva con Neveling von Hardenberg al castello Hardenstein sul fiume Ruhr. Interagiva frequentemente coi mortali.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel it: tinggal bersama Neveling von Hardenberg di kastel Hardenstein, Ruhr.",
        "en": "it article: lived with Neveling von Hardenberg at Hardenstein castle on the Ruhr."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Knucker adalah naga air dalam folklor Sussex, Inggris, yang hidup di kolam dalam bernama knuckerhole.",
    "en": "The knucker is a water dragon of Sussex folklore in England, dwelling in deep pools called knuckerholes.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "knucker-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Knucker adalah kata dialek untuk sejenis naga air yang hidup di knuckerhole di Sussex, Inggris. Kata ini berasal dari bahasa Inggris Kuno nicor, \"monster air\", yang dipakai dalam puisi Beowulf. Knucker dikatakan bersayap dan kadang digambarkan sebagai ular laut. Knuckerhole adalah kolam bundar yang sangat dalam, dianggap tak berdasar; knucker dipercaya ada di Binsted, Lyminster, Lancing, Shoreham dan Worthing. Artikel de menjelaskan kolam itu sebagai mata air di tanah kapur yang suhunya tetap, berkabut tipis saat udara beku.",
      "en": "Knucker is a dialect name for a kind of water dragon living in knuckerholes in Sussex, England. It derives from Old English nicor, 'water monster', a word found in the poem Beowulf. The knucker is said to be winged and is sometimes pictured as a sea serpent. A knuckerhole is a deep round pool thought to have no bottom; knuckers were believed to live at Binsted, Lyminster, Lancing, Shoreham and Worthing. The de article explains these pools as chalk springs of constant temperature that give off thin mist in frosty air.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "knucker-c01",
        "knucker-c02",
        "knucker-c05",
        "knucker-c06",
        "knucker-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Knucker paling terkenal tinggal di Lyminster dan memangsa ternak serta penduduk. Dalam satu versi, seorang ksatria pengembara membunuhnya setelah Raja Sussex menjanjikan putrinya; batu nisan ksatria itu, Slayer's Slab, ada di gereja Lyminster. Versi lain menyebut pemuda petani Jim Pulk (atau Jim Puttock) yang, demi hadiah dari Wali Kota Arundel, membunuh naga itu dengan pai beracun raksasa lalu memenggal kepalanya.",
      "en": "The most famous knucker lived at Lyminster and devoured livestock and villagers. In one version a knight-errant killed it after the King of Sussex promised his daughter to its slayer, and the knight's gravestone, the Slayer's Slab, lies in Lyminster church. Another version credits a farm lad, Jim Pulk or Jim Puttock, who for a reward from the Mayor of Arundel fed the dragon a huge poisoned pie and then cut off its head.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "knucker-c03",
        "knucker-c04"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "knucker-c01",
      "source_id": "knucker-s1",
      "quote": "Knucker is a dialect word for a sort of water dragon, living in knuckerholes in Sussex, England. The word comes from the Old English nicor which means \"water monster\" and is used in the poem Beowulf. It may also be related to the word \"nixie\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga air dialek Sussex, Inggris (England), di knuckerhole; dari kata Inggris Kuno (Old English) nicor, monster air, dalam Beowulf.",
        "en": "A water dragon of Sussex dialect, England, in knuckerholes; from Old English nicor, water monster, used in Beowulf."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knucker-c02",
      "source_id": "knucker-s1",
      "quote": "The knucker is said to have wings, and is sometimes described as a sea serpent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Knucker bersayap, kadang digambarkan sebagai ular laut.",
        "en": "The knucker has wings and is sometimes described as a sea serpent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knucker-c03",
      "source_id": "knucker-s1",
      "quote": "One version has the dragon slain by a knight-errant after the king of Sussex offered his daughter's hand in marriage to whoever rid them of the beast. Legend says that after marrying the princess, the knight settled in Lyminster and his gravestone, the Slayer's Slab, can be seen in Lyminster church.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ksatria pengembara membunuhnya atas janji Raja Sussex; Slayer's Slab di gereja Lyminster; knucker memangsa ternak dan penduduk.",
        "en": "A knight-errant slew it at the King of Sussex's offer; the Slayer's Slab in Lyminster church; it ate livestock and villagers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knucker-c04",
      "source_id": "knucker-s1",
      "quote": "An alternative legend has the dragon outwitted by a local farmer's boy, called Jim Pulk or Jim Puttock, said in some versions to be from Wick, after the Mayor of Arundel offered a reward. He killed the dragon by cooking it a giant poisoned pie",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jim Pulk atau Jim Puttock, hadiah Wali Kota (Mayor) Arundel, membunuh naga dengan pai beracun raksasa dan memenggal kepalanya; dibawa dengan kereta kuda.",
        "en": "Jim Pulk or Jim Puttock, for the Mayor of Arundel's reward, killed the dragon with a giant poisoned pie and beheaded it; brought on a horse and cart."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knucker-c05",
      "source_id": "knucker-s1",
      "quote": "It was believed that knuckers could be found at knuckerholes in various places in Sussex, including Binsted, Lyminster, Lancing, Shoreham and Worthing. A knucker hole is a very deep round pool, which is considered to be infinitely deep.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Knuckerhole di Binsted, Lyminster, Lancing, Shoreham, Worthing; kolam bundar dalam tak berdasar.",
        "en": "Knuckerholes at Binsted, Lyminster, Lancing, Shoreham, Worthing; deep round bottomless pools."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knucker-c06",
      "source_id": "knucker-s2",
      "quote": "Der Knucker ist ein schlangenartiger Wasserdrache aus Sagen in der südenglischen Grafschaft Sussex.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel de: naga air mirip ular dari Sussex; kolam mata air kapur bersuhu tetap, berkabut saat beku.",
        "en": "de article: a serpentine water dragon of Sussex; constant-temperature chalk spring pools that mist in frost."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knucker-c07",
      "source_id": "knucker-s2",
      "quote": "Bei frostigen Lufttemperaturen bildet sich dünner Nebel über dem Wasser, was zur Legendenbildung um die Knucker beigetragen hat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kabut tipis di atas air saat udara beku turut melahirkan legenda knucker.",
        "en": "Thin mist over the water in frost helped give rise to the knucker legends."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lubber fiend atau Lob adalah makhluk legenda folklor Inggris yang mirip brownie, roh rumah yang mengerjakan pekerjaan rumah tangga.",
    "en": "The lubber fiend, or Lob, is a creature of English folklore resembling the brownie, a household spirit that does chores.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lubber-fiend-c01",
      "lubber-fiend-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lubber fiend, juga Lob, lubberkin, lurdane atau Lob Lie-By-The-Fire, adalah makhluk legenda folklor Inggris yang mirip brownie (atau Urisk) Skotlandia, hob Inggris utara, domovoi Slavia dan tomte Skandinavia; ia juga dikaitkan dengan Robin Goodfellow. Ia terkenal karena disebut John Milton dalam puisi pastoral \"L'Allegro\". Biasanya ia digambarkan sebagai lelaki besar berbulu dan berekor yang mengerjakan pekerjaan rumah dengan imbalan semangkuk susu dan tempat di depan perapian.",
      "en": "The lubber fiend, also Lob, lubberkin, lurdane or Lob Lie-By-The-Fire, is a being of English folklore akin to the brownie (or Urisk) of Scotland, the hob of northern England, the Slavic domovoi and the Scandinavian tomte, and has been linked with Robin Goodfellow. John Milton's mention of it in the pastoral 'L'Allegro' is its best-known appearance. It is usually a big hairy man with a tail who does the housework in return for a saucer of milk and a spot by the fire.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lubber-fiend-c01",
        "lubber-fiend-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu kisah menyebutnya putra raksasa seorang penyihir dan Iblis. Abbey lubber adalah setan kecil yang menghantui gudang anggur dan dapur biara, menggoda para biarawan untuk mabuk, rakus dan bernafsu; kisahnya yang paling terkenal adalah Friar Rush. Lob juga muncul dalam karya Edward Thomas, Juliana H. Ewing dan J. M. Barrie, serta dalam The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt sebagai roh pelindung rumah.",
      "en": "One story makes it the giant offspring of a witch and the Devil. The abbey lubber is a lesser demon that infests abbey wine cellars and kitchens, luring monks into drinking, gluttony and lust; Friar Rush is the best-known abbey lubber tale. Lob also turns up in works by Edward Thomas, Juliana H. Ewing and J. M. Barrie, and in The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt as a protective household spirit.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lubber-fiend-c03",
        "lubber-fiend-c04",
        "lubber-fiend-c05",
        "lubber-fiend-c06",

      ]

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Moddey Dhoo (\"anjing hitam\" dalam bahasa Manx) adalah anjing hitam hantu dalam folklor Manx yang konon menghantui Kastel Peel di Pulau Man.",
    "en": "The Moddey Dhoo ('black dog' in Manx) is a phantom black dog of Manx folklore said to haunt Peel Castle on the Isle of Man.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "moddey-dhoo-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Moddey Dhoo, yang dalam bahasa Manx berarti \"anjing hitam\", adalah anjing hantu hitam folklor Manx yang konon menghantui Kastel Peel (Peel Castle) di pesisir barat Pulau Man. Sumber abad ke-18 yang berpengaruh salah menuliskannya menjadi Mauthe Doog. Penyair dan topografer Inggris George Waldron, satu-satunya sumber tertulis utama, menggambarkannya sebagai anjing spaniel hitam besar berbulu keriting yang berbaring di depan perapian ruang jaga di hadapan para prajurit.",
      "en": "The Moddey Dhoo, Manx for 'black dog', is a phantom black hound of Manx folklore reputed to haunt Peel Castle on the Isle of Man's west coast. An influential 18th-century source miswrote the name as Mauthe Doog. The English topographer and poet George Waldron, the only firm written authority, pictured it as a big black spaniel with curly shaggy hair that lay before the guard-room fire in front of the soldiers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "moddey-dhoo-c01",
        "moddey-dhoo-c02",
        "moddey-dhoo-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Anjing itu datang dari sebuah lorong saat senja dan kembali ke sana saat fajar. Menurut Waldron, seorang penjaga yang mabuk nekat mengunci gerbang sendirian dan membawa kunci melalui lorong berhantu; ia kembali ketakutan, tak sanggup bercerita, dan mati tiga hari kemudian. Lorong itu lalu ditutup. Sir Walter Scott memperkenalkannya kepada dunia dalam Peveril of the Peak (1823). Artikel nl menyebut kejadian itu terjadi pada 1666 dan menyamakannya dengan yeth hound Devon dan gwyllgi Wales.",
      "en": "It came out of a passage at dusk and went back into it at dawn. Waldron says a drunken guard insisted on locking the gate alone and took the keys down the haunted passage; he came back terrified, unable to speak of what he saw, and died three days later, after which the passage was sealed. Sir Walter Scott brought the dog to wide notice in Peveril of the Peak (1823). The nl article dates the incident to 1666 and likens it to the yeth hound of Devon and the gwyllgi of Wales.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "moddey-dhoo-c04",
        "moddey-dhoo-c05",
        "moddey-dhoo-c06",
        "moddey-dhoo-c07",
        "moddey-dhoo-c08"
      ]
    }

      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penjaga mabuk kembali ketakutan dan mati tiga hari kemudian; lorong ditutup.",
        "en": "The drunken guard returned terrified and died three days later; the passage was sealed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moddey-dhoo-c06",
      "source_id": "moddey-dhoo-s1",
      "quote": "The dog was made known to the world at large when Sir Walter Scott introduced the \"Manthe Dog -- a fiend, or demon, in the shape of a large, shaggy, black mastiff\" in Peveril of the Peak (1823)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sir Walter Scott dalam Peveril of the Peak (1823).",
        "en": "Sir Walter Scott in Peveril of the Peak (1823)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moddey-dhoo-c07",
      "source_id": "moddey-dhoo-s2",
      "quote": "De beschrijvingen van de Moddey Dhoo vertonen overeenkomsten met vergelijkbare verhalen over hellehonden die tot de overleveringen van de Britse Eilanden behoren, waaronder de yeth hound uit Devon en de gwyllgi uit Wales.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mirip yeth hound Devon dan gwyllgi Wales; artikel nl: kejadian 1666.",
        "en": "Similar to the yeth hound of Devon and gwyllgi of Wales; nl article: incident in 1666."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moddey-dhoo-c08",
      "source_id": "moddey-dhoo-s2",
      "quote": "het door hem beschreven incident zou zich echter in 1666 hebben voorgedaan, kort na de Engelse Burgeroorlog",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Insiden yang dicatat Waldron terjadi pada 1666, setelah Perang Saudara Inggris.",
        "en": "The incident Waldron recorded took place in 1666, after the English Civil War."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Nicnevin adalah penyihir atau ratu peri dalam folklor Skotlandia, sering disamakan dengan Gyre-Carling atau Hecate.",
    "en": "Nicnevin is a witch or fairy queen of Scottish folklore, often equated with the Gyre-Carling or Hecate.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "nicnevin-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nicneven, Nicnevin atau Nicnevan adalah penyihir atau ratu peri dalam folklor Skotlandia. Ia sering dianggap sama dengan Gyre-Carling atau Hecate, meski sebagian sarjana tidak setuju, dan masih diperdebatkan apakah nama itu awalnya merujuk pada perempuan nyata atau dewi. Namanya mungkin dari nama keluarga Gaelik Skotlandia Neachneohain (\"putri yang ilahi\") atau NicNaoimhein (\"putri santo kecil\"), atau dari dewi perang Irlandia Neamhain.",
      "en": "Nicneven, Nicnevin or Nicnevan is a witch or queen of the fairies in Scottish folklore. She is frequently identified with the Gyre-Carling or Hecate, though some scholars dispute this, and it is unclear whether the name first belonged to a real woman or a goddess. It may come from the Scottish Gaelic surname Neachneohain, 'daughter(s) of the divine', or NicNaoimhein, 'daughter of the little saint', or from the Irish war goddess Neamhain.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nicnevin-c01",
        "nicnevin-c02",
        "nicnevin-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penyebutan pertamanya oleh penyair istana Alexander Montgomerie sekitar 1580, dalam flyting melawan Patrick Hume dari Polwarth di bawah Raja James VI: bayi Polwart diserahkan kepada Nicneven dan para penyihirnya, yang mempersembahkannya kepada Hecate dan Pluto. Pada 1801 John Leyden menyebut Nicneven salah satu nama untuk gyre-carlin, Ratu Peri. Robert Cromek menggambarkannya bermantel kelabu panjang dan membawa tongkat yang dapat mengubah air menjadi batu; para ibu menakuti anak-anak dengan ancaman akan menyerahkan mereka kepada McNeven.",
      "en": "She is first named by the court poet Alexander Montgomerie around 1580 in a flyting against Patrick Hume of Polwarth under King James VI, where the infant Polwart is handed to Nicneven and her witches, who devote him to Hecate and Pluto. In 1801 John Leyden listed Nicneven among the names of the gyre-carlin, Queen of Fairies. Robert Cromek described her in a long grey cloak, bearing a wand able to turn water to rock, and said mothers scared children by threatening to give them to McNeven.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nicnevin-c03",
        "nicnevin-c04",

      "source_id": "nicnevin-s1",
      "quote": "The first known mention of Nicneven was by Alexander Montgomerie around 1580. Montgomerie and Patrick Hume of Polwarth were two court poets under King James VI of Scotland.",

      "source_id": "nicnevin-s1",
      "quote": "The infant Polwart is found by the Weird Sisters and then passed on to Nicneven and her group of nymphs or witches, who dedicate him to Hecatus (Hecate) and Pluto in an unholy ritual.",

      "source_id": "nicnevin-s1",

      "source_id": "nicnevin-s1",

  },
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Pearlin Jean adalah hantu yang konon menghantui halaman Allanbank di Allanton, Berwickshire, Skotlandia.",
    "en": "Pearlin Jean is a ghost said to haunt the Allanbank Courtyard at Allanton in Berwickshire, Scotland.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "pearlin-jean-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Pearlin Jean adalah nama hantu yang konon menghantui halaman Allanbank di Allanton, Berwickshire, Skotlandia, bekas lokasi rumah janda di tanah milik Blackadder House yang kini sudah dirobohkan. Namanya berasal dari renda \"pearlin\" yang menutupi tubuhnya menurut para saksi. Menurut legenda ia berasal dari Paris, kekasih Sir Robert Stewart (diangkat Baronet pada 1684). Saat Stewart pulang ke Skotlandia, Jean menghadang keretanya; Stewart menyuruh kusir terus melaju, dan roda kereta melindas dahinya hingga ia tewas. Kata-kata terakhirnya: \"Aku akan tiba di Skotlandia sebelum kamu.\"",
      "en": "Pearlin Jean is the name of a ghost said to haunt the Allanbank Courtyard at Allanton, Berwickshire, Scotland, once the site of a dower house on the estate of the since-demolished Blackadder House. Witnesses gave her the name because she appeared clothed in 'pearlin' lace. Legend makes her a woman from Paris and the lover of Sir Robert Stewart, created a Baronet in 1684. When Stewart left for Scotland she stood in front of his carriage; he told the driver to keep going and the wheels crushed her forehead. Her final words were, 'I'll be in Scotland before you.'",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pearlin-jean-c01",
        "pearlin-jean-c02",
        "pearlin-jean-c03",
        "pearlin-jean-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ketika pertama kali kembali ke tanah miliknya, sebelum 1697, Stewart melihatnya duduk di atas gerbang dengan darah menutupi kepala dan bahunya. Tujuh pendeta setempat gagal mengusirnya. Hantu itu membanting pintu, membuka jendela dan berjalan di rumah siang malam; lama-kelamaan wujudnya menjadi kerangka berbalut renda. Pada abad ke-18 Thomas Blackadder ketakutan saat menemuinya, dan pada 1790 para tamu perempuan terganggu suara langkah kaki. Legendanya diabadikan dalam lagu pada abad ke-19.",
      "en": "On Stewart's first return to the estate, before 1697, he saw her perched above the gateway with blood covering her head and shoulders. Seven local ministers failed to exorcise her. The ghost banged doors, opened windows and roamed the house at all hours, and in time took the form of a skeleton in lace. In the 18th century Thomas Blackadder was terrified on meeting her, and in 1790 lady guests were troubled by pacing footsteps. A 19th-century song preserved the legend.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pearlin-jean-c04",
        "pearlin-jean-c05",
        "pearlin-jean-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "Pearlin Jean is the name given to a ghost, who purportedly haunts the Allanbank Courtyard, situated in Allanton, Berwickshire, Scotland. The courtyard was previously the site of a dower house called Allanbank of the country estate of the now demolished Blackadder House.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pearlin Jean, hantu di halaman Allanbank (Allanbank Courtyard), Allanton, Berwickshire, Skotlandia (Scotland); bekas rumah janda tanah Blackadder House.",
        "en": "Pearlin Jean, a ghost of the Allanbank Courtyard, Allanton, Berwickshire, Scotland; former dower house of Blackadder House."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pearlin-jean-c02",
      "source_id": "pearlin-jean-s1",
      "quote": "The ghost's name is derived from the eye-witness' claim that she is seen covered in the similarly named lace",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama dari renda (lace) pearlin yang menutupinya.",
        "en": "Named from the pearlin lace covering her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pearlin-jean-c03",
      "source_id": "pearlin-jean-s1",
      "quote": "Jean attempted to physically stop Stewart from leaving her by stepping out in front of his carriage. He ordered his driver to carry on regardless, and the woman fell. The wheels of the carriage then ran over her forehead, killing her.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jean menghadang kereta Stewart dan tewas dilindas; kata terakhir: I'll be in Scotland before you; berasal dari Paris.",
        "en": "Jean stepped in front of Stewart's carriage and was killed; last words: I'll be in Scotland before you; from Paris."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pearlin-jean-c04",
      "source_id": "pearlin-jean-s1",
      "quote": "he saw his ex-lover sitting on top of the entrance to the estate, with blood covering her head and shoulders. This first sighting was at least prior to 1697.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Stewart melihatnya di atas gerbang berlumur darah, sebelum 1697.",
        "en": "Stewart saw her atop the entrance covered in blood, before 1697."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pearlin-jean-c05",
      "source_id": "pearlin-jean-s1",
      "quote": "The ghost was said to slam doors, open windows and walk throughout the house at all times of the day and night. As time progressed, Pearlin Jean's ghostly figure became that of a skeleton in Pearlin lace",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hantu membanting pintu, membuka jendela; kemudian berwujud kerangka berbalut renda Pearlin; tujuh pendeta gagal mengusirnya.",
        "en": "The ghost slammed doors and opened windows; later a skeleton in Pearlin lace; seven ministers failed to exorcise it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pearlin-jean-c06",
      "source_id": "pearlin-jean-s1",
      "quote": "In the 1700s, Thomas Blackadder was said to be infatuated with the figure of Pearlin Jean, and went to meet her in the courtyard. As soon as he realised the nature of the woman, he became incredibly frightened.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Thomas Blackadder ketakutan pada abad ke-18; 1790 suara langkah kaki; lagu abad ke-19.",
        "en": "Thomas Blackadder was frightened in the 18th century; footsteps in 1790; 19th-century song."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pearlin-jean-c07",
      "source_id": "pearlin-jean-s2",
      "quote": "Según la leyenda, fue amante de Sir Robert Stewart (quien fue nombrado baronet en 1684) y monja de la caridad que no estaba confinada a un claustro.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kekasih Sir Robert Stewart, diangkat Baronet (baronet) pada 1684.",
        "en": "Lover of Sir Robert Stewart, made a Baronet in 1684."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Pictish Beast adalah gambaran hewan yang khas dalam budaya Pikt di Skotlandia pada awal Abad Pertengahan, terutama pada batu-batu Pikt.",
    "en": "The Pictish Beast is a stylised animal image peculiar to the early medieval Picts of Scotland, found mostly on Pictish stones.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "pictish-beast-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Pictish Beast (kadang Pictish Dragon atau Pictish Elephant) adalah representasi hewan yang khas dalam budaya Pikt di Skotlandia pada awal Abad Pertengahan, sebagian besar pada batu Pikt. Satu contoh juga terdapat di Doo Cave, East Wemyss, yang dicatat Anderson pada 1881. Menurut artikel de, simbol ini dibuat antara abad ke-7 dan ke-9 dan muncul 29 kali pada batu Kelas I, 25 kali pada batu Kelas II, dan lima kali di dinding gua.",
      "en": "The Pictish Beast (sometimes Pictish Dragon or Pictish Elephant) is a stylised animal unique to the early medieval Picts of Scotland, and most surviving examples are carved on Pictish stones. One example also appears in the Doo Cave at East Wemyss, noted by Anderson in 1881. The de article dates the figures to the 7th to 9th centuries and counts 29 occurrences on Class I stones, 25 on Class II stones and five on cave walls.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pictish-beast-c01",
        "pictish-beast-c02",
        "pictish-beast-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hewan apa yang digambarkan masih belum jelas. Jill Harden menulis bahwa sosok itu jelas sedang berenang dan bertanya apakah ia lumba-lumba, pesut, atau makhluk mitos. Identifikasi lain yang pernah diusulkan antara lain gajah, kelpie (each uisge) dan kuda laut. Artikel de menyebut ciri khasnya: moncong panjang seperti paruh, ujung kaki yang menggulung, jambul panjang dari belakang kepala, dan ekor menggantung.",
      "en": "What animal it shows is unresolved. Jill Harden writes that the figure is plainly swimming and asks whether it is a porpoise, a dolphin or a mythical creature. Other proposals include an elephant, a kelpie (each uisge) and a seahorse. The de article lists its marks: a long beak-like snout, curled limb ends, a long lappet trailing from the back of the head, and a hanging tail.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pictish-beast-c03",
        "pictish-beast-c04",
        "pictish-beast-c06"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Seonaidh (Shony) adalah roh air di Pulau Lewis, Skotlandia, menurut Martin Martin, yang diberi persembahan ale agar mengirim rumput laut.",
    "en": "The Seonaidh (Shony) was a water spirit of Lewis in Scotland, per Martin Martin, offered ale in return for seaweed.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "seonaidh-c01",
      "seonaidh-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Seonaidh (dibakukan dalam bahasa Inggris sebagai Shony atau Shoney) adalah roh air di Lewis menurut Martin Martin. Kamus Dwelly mendefinisikan bentuk terkait seonadh sebagai \"tenung, sihir\" dan \"Druidisme\". Penduduk Lewis berkumpul di gereja St. Mulway (Mael rubha), setiap keluarga menyumbang sekantong malt, dan semuanya diseduh menjadi ale.",
      "en": "The Seonaidh (anglicised Shony or Shoney) was a water spirit of Lewis according to Martin Martin. Dwelly's dictionary glosses the related form seonadh as 'augury, sorcery' and 'Druidism'. The people of Lewis would gather at the church of St. Mulway (Mael rubha), each family giving a bag of malt, which was all brewed into ale.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "seonaidh-c01",
        "seonaidh-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seorang dipilih untuk berjalan ke laut sampai sepinggang membawa secawan ale, lalu berseru memohon Seonaidh mengirim banyak rumput laut untuk menyuburkan tanah, dan menuangkan ale ke laut; upacara ini dilakukan malam hari. Kemungkinan Seonaidh semula semacam dewa yang pemujaannya sedikit dikristenkan, atau namanya, bentuk Gaelik Skotlandia untuk Johnny, merujuk pada salah satu Santo Yohanes.",
      "en": "One man was picked to wade waist-deep into the sea with a cup of ale, call on Seonaidh to send plenty of seaweed to enrich the land, and pour the ale into the waves, all done at night. Seonaidh may once have been a god whose cult was lightly Christianised, or the name, the Scottish Gaelic form of Johnny, may point to one of the Saints John.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "seonaidh-c03",
        "seonaidh-c04",
        "seonaidh-c05",
        "seonaidh-c06",
        "seonaidh-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "seonaidh-c02",
      "source_id": "seonaidh-s1",
      "quote": "They came to the church of St. Mulway (Mael rubha ), each man carrying his own provisions. Every family gave a pock (bag) of malt, and the whole was brewed into ale.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kamus Dwelly: seonadh = tenung, sihir, Druidisme; penduduk Lewis berkumpul di gereja St. Mulway (Mael rubha), malt diseduh menjadi ale.",
        "en": "Dwelly: seonadh = augury, sorcery, Druidism; Lewis folk met at the church of St. Mulway (Mael rubha), malt brewed into ale."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seonaidh-c03",
      "source_id": "seonaidh-s1",
      "quote": "I give thee this cup of ale, hoping that thou wilt be so good as to send us plenty of seaware [seaweed used as a fertilizer] for enriching our ground during the coming year.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Persembahan ale kepada Seonaidh untuk rumput laut penyubur tanah.",
        "en": "Ale offered to Seonaidh for seaweed to enrich the ground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seonaidh-c04",
      "source_id": "seonaidh-s1",
      "quote": "He then threw the ale into the sea, in a ceremony performed at night.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ale dilempar ke laut dalam upacara malam.",
        "en": "The ale thrown into the sea in a night ceremony."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seonaidh-c05",
      "source_id": "seonaidh-s1",
      "quote": "It seems likely that Seonaidh was originally some kind of god, whose worship had been lightly Christianised by the addition of various church features.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seonaidh mungkin semula dewa yang pemujaannya dikristenkan.",
        "en": "Seonaidh was likely once a god whose cult was Christianised."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seonaidh-c06",
      "source_id": "seonaidh-s1",
      "quote": "However, it is also possible that Seonaidh , the Scottish Gaelic form of the English Johnny, may also be a reference to one of the Saints John.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seonaidh bentuk Gaelik Skotlandia (Scottish Gaelic) untuk Johnny, mungkin merujuk Santo Yohanes (Saints John).",
        "en": "Seonaidh, Scottish Gaelic for Johnny, may refer to one of the Saints John."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seonaidh-c07",
      "source_id": "seonaidh-s2",
      "quote": "Lewis halkı ekinlerinin çok mahsul vermesi için bu su cinine bir çeşit bira olan ale adarlardı.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel tr: penduduk Lewis mempersembahkan ale kepada roh air ini demi panen.",
        "en": "tr article: the people of Lewis offered ale to this water spirit for good harvests."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",

    {

    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sheka adalah makhluk mirip kurcaci dalam mitologi Turkik yang tinggal di hutan atau rumah terbengkalai; dalam paganisme Slavia ia roh ladang.",
    "en": "Sheka is a dwarf-like being of Turkic myth living in forests or abandoned houses; in Slavic paganism it is a field spirit.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sheka-mythology-c01",
      "sheka-mythology-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sheka (Sirilik: Шекә) adalah makhluk mirip kurcaci dalam mitologi Turkik yang tinggal di hutan atau rumah terbengkalai; dalam legenda ia biasanya muncul dalam situasi lucu. Artikel ca menyebutnya makhluk mitologi Tatar dengan nama Şekä, dan Çike dalam bahasa Turki.",
      "en": "Sheka (Cyrillic Шекә) is a dwarf-like being of Turkic mythology that inhabits forests or deserted houses and usually turns up in comic situations in the legends. The ca article places it in Tatar mythology as Şekä, called Çike in Turkish.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sheka-mythology-c01",
        "sheka-mythology-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam paganisme Slavia, Sheka adalah roh ladang berwujud kurcaci cacat dengan mata berbeda warna dan rumput sebagai rambut, yang muncul pada tengah hari atau senja dengan pakaian serba hitam atau serba putih. Ia menyesatkan pengembara di ladang, menularkan penyakit, atau menginjak orang yang tertidur dengan kuda; ia suka menarik rambut petani yang bekerja di siang hari, membuat anak-anak tersesat di ladang jagung, dan memaksa orang yang ditangkapnya bernyanyi berjam-jam.",
      "en": "In Slavic paganism Sheka is a field spirit in the shape of a misshapen dwarf with eyes of different colours and grass for hair, appearing at noon or sunset dressed wholly in black or wholly in white. It leads wanderers astray in the fields, spreads illness, or rides horses over people found asleep; it likes tugging the hair of peasants at work at midday, gets small children lost among the corn, and makes anyone it catches sing for hours.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sheka-mythology-c02",
        "sheka-mythology-c03"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sihirtia (Sirtia) adalah bangsa mitos bertubuh sangat pendek dalam folklor Nenets yang tinggal di tundra sebelum kedatangan orang Nenets.",
    "en": "The Sihirtia (Sirtia) are a mythical, very short people of Nenets folklore who lived in the tundra before the Nenets came.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sihirtia-c01",
      "sihirtia-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sihirtia (Nenets: сихиртя) atau Sirtia (сиртя) adalah bangsa mitos yang mendiami tundra sebelum kedatangan orang Nenets, dan namanya sering muncul dalam folklor Nenets. Menurut legenda mereka sangat pendek, berpakaian indah dengan liontin logam, dan tinggal di bukit pasir tinggi. Mereka keluar ke permukaan tundra pada malam hari atau saat berkabut; di bawah tanah mereka bepergian dengan anjing dan menggembalakan mamut (\"rusa tanah\"). Artikel ru menyebut mereka takut cahaya siang.",
      "en": "The Sihirtia (Nenets сихиртя) or Sirtia (сиртя) were a mythical people who held the tundra before the Nenets arrived, and they figure often in Nenets folklore. Legend makes them very short, finely dressed with metal pendants, and living in tall sandy hills. They emerged on the tundra at night or in fog; underground they rode on dogs and herded mammoths, the 'earth deer'. The ru article adds that they fear daylight.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sihirtia-c01",
        "sihirtia-c02",
        "sihirtia-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sihirtia adalah pandai besi yang terampil dan prajurit yang baik; bertemu mereka membawa sial atau untung, dan ada kisah pernikahan antara orang Nenets dan Sihirtia. Gambaran folklor ini mungkin menyimpan ciri bangsa nyata, kemungkinan berasal dari rumpun Samoyed atau Paleo-Siberia, yang tinggal di tundra Eropa dan Siberia Barat pada zaman kuno.",
      "en": "The Sihirtia were able smiths and good fighters; meeting one brought either bad or good luck, and Nenets and Sihirtia are said to have intermarried. The folk image may preserve traits of an actual people, perhaps Samoyedic or Paleo-Siberian, who lived in the European and West Siberian tundra in ancient times.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sihirtia-c03",
        "sihirtia-c04",
        "sihirtia-c05"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [

      "id": "sihirtia-c01",
      "source_id": "sihirtia-s1",
      "quote": "Sihirtia (Nenets: сихиртя ) or Sirtia (Nenets: сиртя ) were a mythical people who lived in the tundra before the arrival of the Nenets. The name is often encountered in the folklore of the Nenets.",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Sihirtia (сихиртя) atau Sirtia (сиртя), bangsa mitos tundra sebelum orang Nenets; folklor Nenets.",
        "en": "Sihirtia (сихиртя) or Sirtia (сиртя), a mythical people of the tundra before the Nenets; Nenets folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sihirtia-c02",
      "source_id": "sihirtia-s1",
      "quote": "Tall sandy hills served them as homes. They came out to the surface of the tundra at night or in the mist; they lived under the ground, where they traveled on dogs and pastured mammoths (\"earth deer\").",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sangat pendek, berpakaian indah berliontin logam; tinggal di bukit pasir; keluar malam atau saat berkabut; di bawah tanah naik anjing dan menggembala mamut (rusa tanah).",
        "en": "Very short, finely dressed with metal pendants; live in sandy hills; emerge at night or in mist; underground ride dogs and herd mammoths (earth deer)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sihirtia-c03",
      "source_id": "sihirtia-s1",
      "quote": "Sihirtia were skilled smiths and good warriors.",

      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "sihirtia-c04",
      "source_id": "sihirtia-s1",
      "quote": "Meeting a Sihirtia brought either misfortune or good luck. There were cases of marriage between Nenets and Sihirtia.",

      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "sihirtia-c05",
      "source_id": "sihirtia-s1",
      "quote": "The folklore image of Sihirtia might have mythologically imprinted features of a real people (probably of Samoyedic or Paleo-Siberian origin) who lived in Europe and West Siberian tundra in antiquity.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Monster Danau Thetis adalah makhluk legenda dan tipuan yang diakui dari Victoria, British Columbia, Kanada, yang dilaporkan muncul pada 1972.",
    "en": "The Thetis Lake Monster is a legendary creature and confessed hoax from Victoria, British Columbia, Canada, reported in 1972.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "thetis-lake-monster-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Monster Danau Thetis adalah makhluk legenda sekaligus tipuan yang diakui dari Victoria, British Columbia, Kanada. Pada 1972 dua remaja lelaki mengaku melihat monster keluar dari pantai Danau Thetis. Menurut Victoria Daily Times (22 Agustus 1972), makhluk itu berkulit sisik keperakan, bercakar tajam dan berduri di kepala, mirip Gill-man dari film Creature from the Black Lagoon (1954). Artikel fi menyebutnya manusia kadal dengan insang dan paru-paru serta tangan dan kaki berselaput.",
      "en": "The Thetis Lake Monster is a legendary creature, and an admitted hoax, from Victoria, British Columbia, Canada. In 1972 two teenage boys said they saw a monster come out of the water at Thetis Lake beach. The Victoria Daily Times of 22 August 1972 reported silvery scales, sharp claws and spikes on its head, roughly like the Gill-man of the 1954 film Creature from the Black Lagoon. The fi article calls it a lizard-man with gills and lungs and webbed hands and feet.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "thetis-lake-monster-c01",
        "thetis-lake-monster-c02",
        "thetis-lake-monster-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Polisi Berkuda Kerajaan Kanada (Royal Canadian Mounted Police) menyelidiki tetapi tidak menemukan bukti. Bertahun-tahun kemudian penulis Daniel Loxton menghubungi salah satu saksi, yang mengaku cerita itu dikarang untuk mencari perhatian. Loxton menemukan bahwa gambaran monster itu persis sama dengan monster dalam film Monster From The Surf, yang ditayangkan televisi setempat beberapa hari sebelumnya.",
      "en": "The Royal Canadian Mounted Police looked into it but found nothing. Years afterwards the writer Daniel Loxton reached one of the witnesses, who confessed the tale had been made up for attention. Loxton found that the creature described was an exact match for the monster of the film Monster From The Surf, shown on local television a few days before.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "thetis-lake-monster-c03",
        "thetis-lake-monster-c04",
        "thetis-lake-monster-c06"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Thunderdell adalah raksasa berkepala dua dari Cornwall yang dibunuh Jack the Giant-Killer dalam kisah-kisah Tabart dan lainnya.",
    "en": "Thunderdell is a Cornish giant with two heads whom Jack the Giant-Killer kills in Tabart's tales and other versions.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "thunderdell-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Thunderdell (Welsh: Taranau), juga dicatat sebagai Thunderdel, Thunderel, Thundrel, Thunderdale atau Thunderbore, adalah raksasa berkepala dua dari Cornwall yang dibunuh Jack the Giant-Killer dalam kisah Tabart dan lainnya. Dalam Jack the Giant Killer ia menyerbu jamuan yang disiapkan untuk Jack sambil melantunkan \"fee fau fum\"; Jack mengalahkan dan memenggal kedua kepalanya dengan tipu daya memakai parit dan jembatan angkat rumah itu.",
      "en": "Thunderdell (Welsh Taranau) appears under many spellings, among them Thunderdel, Thunderel, Thundrel, Thunderdale and Thunderbore. He is a two-headed Cornish giant killed by Jack the Giant-Killer in Tabart's version and others. In Jack the Giant Killer he bursts in on a feast laid on for Jack, chanting 'fee fau fum', and Jack beats him and cuts off his heads by a ruse with the house's moat and drawbridge.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "thunderdell-c01",
        "thunderdell-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam satu versi tahun 1800, ia disebut \"Thunderful\" dan berasal dari Kutub Utara; ia menyerang jamuan Jack untuk membalas kematian dua raksasa yang telah dibunuh Jack, tetapi kalah dan kepalanya dikirim ke istana Raja Arthur. Nama Thunderdell juga dipakai untuk raksasa dalam film Jack and the Beanstalk: The Real Story, diperankan Bill Barretta.",
      "en": "In a version from 1800 he is called 'Thunderful' and comes from the North Pole; he raids Jack's feast to avenge two giants Jack had killed, but is beaten and his heads are sent to King Arthur's court. The name Thunderdell was also given to a giant in Jack and the Beanstalk: The Real Story, played by Bill Barretta.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "thunderdell-c03",
        "thunderdell-c04"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [

      "id": "thunderdell-c01",
      "source_id": "thunderdell-s1",
      "quote": "Thunderdell (Welsh: Taranau ), also recorded as Thunderdel, Thunderel, Thundrel, Thunderdale, or Thunderbore, was a two-headed giant of Cornwall slain by Jack the Giant-Killer in the stories of Tabart and others.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Thunderdell (Welsh: Taranau; Thunderdel, Thunderel, Thundrel, Thunderdale, Thunderbore), raksasa berkepala dua Cornwall dibunuh Jack the Giant-Killer (Jack the Giant Killer) dalam kisah Tabart.",
        "en": "Thunderdell (Welsh: Taranau; Thunderdel, Thunderel, Thundrel, Thunderdale, Thunderbore), a two-headed Cornish giant slain by Jack the Giant-Killer in Tabart's stories."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "thunderdell-c02",
      "source_id": "thunderdell-s1",
      "quote": "In Jack the Giant Killer, Thunderdell first appeared where he crashed a banquet that was prepared for Jack. During this time, he chanted \"fee fau fum.\" Jack defeats and beheads the two-headed giant with a trick involving the house's moat and drawbridge.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menyerbu jamuan Jack, melantunkan fee fau fum; dipenggal dengan tipu parit dan jembatan angkat.",
        "en": "Crashed Jack's banquet chanting fee fau fum; beheaded via the moat and drawbridge trick."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "thunderdell-c03",
      "source_id": "thunderdell-s1",
      "quote": "According to one version of the story from 1800, Thunderdell (here identified as \"Thunderful\") hails from the North Pole. He attacks Jack's banquet in order to avenge the deaths of two giants he had earlier slain, but is himself defeated and his heads sent to the court of King Arthur.",
      "locator": null,
