        "id": "Versi 1800: Thunderful dari Kutub Utara (North Pole); kepalanya dikirim ke istana Raja Arthur (King Arthur).",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Aobh adalah tokoh perempuan dalam mitologi Kelt Irlandia, putri Bodb Dearg, istri Lir, dan ibu Anak-anak Lir.",
    "en": "Aobh is a female figure of Irish Celtic myth, daughter of Bodb Dearg, wife of Lir and mother of the Children of Lir.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "aobh-c01",
      "aobh-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Aobh (juga Áeb atau Aebh) adalah tokoh perempuan dalam mitologi Kelt Irlandia. Ia putri Bodb Dearg dan istri Lir, raja Túatha Dé Danann. Setelah dua kali melahirkan anak kembar, Fionnuala dan Aed lalu Fiachra dan Conn, ia meninggal saat melahirkan. Menurut artikel cs, kisah Oidheadh Chloinne Lir yang dicatat pada abad ke-15 menyebut Bodb Derg dari Connacht menawarkan Lir salah satu putri angkatnya, dan yang tertua, Áeb, dipilih.",
      "en": "Aobh (also Áeb or Aebh) is a female figure of Irish Celtic mythology, the daughter of Bodb Dearg and wife of Lir, king of the Túatha Dé Danann. After bearing two sets of twins, Fionnuala and Aed and then Fiachra and Conn, she died in childbirth. The cs article notes that in the Oidheadh Chloinne Lir, written down in the 15th century, Bodb Derg of Connacht offered Lir one of his foster daughters, and the eldest, Áeb, was chosen.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aobh-c01",
        "aobh-c02",
        "aobh-c04",
        "aobh-c07",
        "aobh-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Saudarinya Aoife, istri kedua Lir, ingin anak-anak Aobh dibunuh, tetapi seorang pelayan yang iba justru mengubah mereka menjadi angsa; dalam versi lain Aoife sendiri, karena menyesal, yang mengubah mereka. Kisah ini dikenal sebagai Oidheadh Chlainne Lir (\"Kisah Anak-anak Lir\"). Dalam versi lain lagi, Aobh menikah dengan Manannan mac Lir, putra Lir.",
      "en": "Her sister Aoife, Lir's second wife, wanted Aobh's children killed, but a servant took pity and turned them into swans instead; another version has a remorseful Aoife transform them herself. The story survives as Oidheadh Chlainne Lir ('The Tale of the Children of Lir'). In yet another version Aobh is married to Manannan mac Lir, Lir's son.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aobh-c03",

        "aobh-c06"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "aobh-c01",
      "source_id": "aobh-s1",
      "quote": "Aobh ist eine weibliche Sagengestalt aus der keltischen Mythologie Irlands.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aobh, tokoh perempuan mitologi Kelt Irlandia (Irlands).",
        "en": "Aobh, a female figure of Irish Celtic mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aobh-c02",
      "source_id": "aobh-s1",
      "quote": "Sie ist die Tochter Bodb Deargs und die Gattin Lirs, des Königs der Túatha Dé Danann. Nachdem sie ihm zweimal Zwillinge geboren hat, stirbt sie im Kindbett.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Putri Bodb Dearg, istri Lir, raja Túatha Dé Danann; dua kali melahirkan kembar (Fionnuala, Aed, Fiachra, Conn), meninggal saat melahirkan; Anak-anak (Children of) Lir.",
        "en": "Daughter of Bodb Dearg, wife of Lir, king of the Túatha Dé Danann; bore twins twice (Fionnuala, Aed, Fiachra, Conn), died in childbirth; the Children of Lir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aobh-c03",
      "source_id": "aobh-s1",
      "quote": "Ihre Kinder, Fionnuala, Aed, Fiachra und Conn, sollen nach dem Willen ihrer Schwester Aoife, der zweiten Gemahlin Lirs, getötet werden, stattdessen verwandelt sie eine mitleidige Dienerin in Schwäne.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anak-anaknya hendak dibunuh atas kehendak saudarinya Aoife, istri kedua Lir; pelayan mengubah mereka menjadi angsa.",
        "en": "Her children were to be killed at the will of her sister Aoife, Lir's second wife; a servant turned them into swans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aobh-c04",
      "source_id": "aobh-s2",
      "quote": "Áeb, též Aobh či Aebh, je postava z irské mytologie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Áeb (Aobh, Aebh), tokoh mitologi Irlandia.",
        "en": "Áeb (Aobh, Aebh), a figure of Irish mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aobh-c05",
      "source_id": "aobh-s1",
      "quote": "Nach einer anderen Version soll Aoife, von Reue gepackt, die Kinder selbst verwandelt haben. Diese Erzählung ist als Oidheadh Chlainne Lir („Die Geschichte der Kinder Lirs“) überliefert.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain: Aoife yang menyesal mengubah anak-anak itu sendiri; kisah Oidheadh Chlainne Lir.",
        "en": "Another version: a remorseful Aoife transformed them herself; the tale Oidheadh Chlainne Lir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aobh-c06",
      "source_id": "aobh-s1",
      "quote": "In einer anderen Version heiratet sie Manannan mac Lir, den Sohn Lirs.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain: menikah dengan Manannan mac Lir, putra Lir.",
        "en": "Another version: she marries Manannan mac Lir, Lir's son."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aobh-c07",
      "source_id": "aobh-s2",
      "quote": "Vybrána je ta nejstarší, jménem Áeb, a ta Lirovi porodí jedny dvojčata, dceru Finngualu a syna Áeda, a poté další dvojčata Fiachru a Conna, při jejich porodu však zemře.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Áeb, putri tertua yang dipilih, melahirkan Finnguala dan Áed, lalu Fiachra dan Conn, dan meninggal saat melahirkan.",
        "en": "Áeb, the eldest chosen, bore Finnguala and Áed, then Fiachra and Conn, and died in childbirth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aobh-c08",
      "source_id": "aobh-s2",
      "quote": "Podle vyprávění Oidheadh Chloinne Lir „Tragického příběhu o dětech Lirových“ zaznamenaného v 15. století byl po bitvě u Tailtiu zvolen králem Tuatha Dé Danann Bodb Derg z Connachtu",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Oidheadh Chloinne Lir yang dicatat abad ke-15, Bodb Derg dari Connacht dipilih menjadi raja Tuatha Dé Danann.",
        "en": "Per the Oidheadh Chloinne Lir, recorded in the 15th century, Bodb Derg of Connacht was chosen king of the Tuatha Dé Danann."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Asrai adalah sejenis peri air dalam folklor dan sastra Inggris, biasanya perempuan yang tinggal di danau; mungkin ciptaan penyair Robert Williams Buchanan.",
    "en": "The asrai is a kind of water fairy in English folklore and literature, usually female and lake-dwelling; it may be an invention of the poet Robert Williams Buchanan.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "asrai-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Asrai adalah sejenis peri air dalam folklor dan sastra Inggris, biasanya digambarkan perempuan, tinggal di danau, dan mirip putri duyung serta nixie. Etimologinya tidak diketahui; ejaan Asrey atau ashray kadang muncul. Kemunculan cetak tertuanya adalah puisi \"The Asrai\" karya penyair Skotlandia Robert Williams Buchanan, terbit April 1872, sehingga asrai mungkin ciptaannya sendiri. Dalam puisi Buchanan, asrai adalah makhluk pucat dan lembut, lebih tua dari manusia, yang takut cahaya dan hidup di bawah danau.",
      "en": "The asrai is a type of water fairy in English folklore and literature, generally female, living in lakes and resembling the mermaid and the nixie. Nobody knows where the word comes from; it is sometimes spelled Asrey or ashray. Its earliest known printed appearance is 'The Asrai', a poem by the Scottish poet Robert Williams Buchanan first published in April 1872, so the being may be his own creation. Buchanan's asrai are pale, gentle creatures older than mankind who shun light and live under a lake.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "asrai-c01",
        "asrai-c02",
        "asrai-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asrai kembali dikenal lewat karya pencerita Inggris Ruth Tongue, yang keandalannya sebagai folkloris diragukan. Tongue mengaitkan kisahnya dengan Cheshire, Shropshire dan perbatasan Wales: asrai pemalu, sangat cantik, berkaki selaput dan berambut hijau, hidup ratusan tahun dan muncul ke permukaan sekali seabad untuk mandi cahaya bulan. Seorang nelayan yang menangkap asrai terbakar kulitnya oleh sentuhan tangannya yang dingin, dan asrai itu mencair menjadi genangan air karena terlalu lama terkena matahari.",
      "en": "Renewed interest came through the British storyteller Ruth Tongue, whose reliability as a folklorist has been doubted. She located asrai tales in Cheshire, Shropshire and the Welsh Border: shy, very beautiful beings with webbed feet and green hair, living for centuries and rising to the surface once every hundred years to bathe in moonlight. A fisherman who caught one was burned by the touch of its cold hands, and the asrai melted into a puddle because it had been in the sun too long.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "asrai-c04",
        "asrai-c05",
        "asrai-c06",
        "asrai-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did_you_know": {
    "id": "Asrai yang ditangkap dan terlalu lama terkena matahari konon mencair, hanya meninggalkan genangan air.",
    "en": "An asrai that is caught and left in the sun too long is said to melt, leaving only a puddle.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "asrai-c01",
      "source_id": "asrai-s1",
      "quote": "The asrai is a type of aquatic fairy in English folklore and literature. They are usually depicted as female, live in lakes and are similar to the mermaid and nixie. Rather than originating from folklore, the asrai may have been invented by the Scottish poet Robert Williams Buchanan.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asrai, peri air folklor dan sastra Inggris (English), perempuan, tinggal di danau, mirip putri duyung dan nixie; mungkin ciptaan penyair Skotlandia (Scottish) Robert Williams Buchanan.",
        "en": "The asrai, an aquatic fairy of English folklore and literature, female, lake-dwelling, like the mermaid and nixie; possibly invented by Scottish poet Robert Williams Buchanan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "asrai-c02",
      "source_id": "asrai-s1",
      "quote": "\"Asrey\" or \"ashray\" sometimes appear as spelling variants. It is unclear whether the asrai was ever part of folk belief. Their oldest known appearance in print was the poem \"The Asrai\" by Robert Williams Buchanan, first published in April 1872",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ejaan Asrey atau ashray; puisi The Asrai karya Robert Williams Buchanan, April 1872.",
        "en": "Spellings Asrey or ashray; the poem The Asrai by Robert Williams Buchanan, April 1872."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "asrai-c03",
      "source_id": "asrai-s1",
      "quote": "In Buchanan's poetry, the asrai are pale, gentle beings, older than humanity, who fear light and live beneath a lake.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam puisi Buchanan: pucat, lembut, lebih tua dari manusia, takut cahaya, hidup di bawah danau.",
        "en": "In Buchanan's poetry: pale, gentle, older than humanity, fearing light, living under a lake."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "asrai-c04",
      "source_id": "asrai-s1",
      "quote": "The asrai gained renewed attention through the works of British storyteller Ruth Tongue, whose reliability as a folklorist has been questioned.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ruth Tongue, pencerita Inggris (British), keandalannya diragukan.",
        "en": "Ruth Tongue, British storyteller of questioned reliability."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "asrai-c05",
      "source_id": "asrai-s1",
      "quote": "By the time the fisherman reached the shore, the asrai had melted away leaving nothing but a puddle of water in the boat, for the asrai perish if directly exposed too long to the sun.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asrai mencair menjadi genangan air bila terlalu lama terkena matahari.",
        "en": "The asrai melted into a puddle when exposed too long to the sun."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "asrai-c06",
      "source_id": "asrai-s1",
      "quote": "Ruth Tongue attributed stories of the asrai to Cheshire, Shropshire, and the Welsh Border. In her collected work, the asrai are timid and shy, very beautiful, and have webbed feet and green hair.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ruth Tongue: Cheshire, Shropshire, perbatasan Wales (Welsh Border); pemalu, cantik, kaki berselaput, rambut hijau; muncul sekali seabad mandi cahaya bulan; nelayan terbakar sentuhannya.",
        "en": "Ruth Tongue: Cheshire, Shropshire, the Welsh Border; shy, beautiful, webbed feet, green hair; surfaces once a century to bathe in moonlight; a fisherman burned by its touch."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "asrai-c07",
      "source_id": "asrai-s2",
      "quote": "Om en Asrai nuddar solljuset eller fångas så dör den, och faller ihop i en vattenpöl.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel sv: asrai yang tersentuh sinar matahari atau ditangkap akan mati menjadi genangan air.",
        "en": "sv article: an asrai touched by sunlight or caught dies and collapses into a puddle."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Baloma adalah roh orang mati dalam masyarakat Trobriand, yang diyakini membuat perempuan mengandung dan menjaga garis keturunan matrilineal.",
    "en": "Baloma is the spirit of the dead in Trobriand society, believed to make women conceive and so sustain matrilineal descent.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "baloma-c01",
      "baloma-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Baloma adalah roh orang mati dalam masyarakat Kepulauan Trobriand, sebagaimana dikaji Bronislaw Malinowski pada awal abad ke-20 dan dipublikasikannya pada 1916. Roh ini berperan penting dalam pandangan tentang pembuahan: ia menjelaskan dan menopang sistem keturunan matrilineal dengan menggantikan peran sperma laki-laki (\"ayah\") dengan peran roh.",
      "en": "Baloma is the spirit of the dead among the people of the Trobriand Islands, as studied by Bronislaw Malinowski in the early 20th century and published by him in 1916. It is central to local ideas of conception, explaining and upholding the matrilineal descent system by giving to a spirit the part otherwise played by male sperm ('fathers').",
      "claim_ids": [
        "baloma-c01",
        "baloma-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Baloma adalah roh anggota garis keturunan yang telah meninggal, yang membuahi perempuan ketika mereka mandi di laut, sehingga garis keturunan ibu terus berlanjut. Hubungan seksual hanya dipandang sebagai cara membuka jalan bagi baloma memasuki rahim. Artikel pl menegaskan bahwa kepercayaan ini menjadi dasar sistem pewarisan matrilineal orang Trobriand.",
      "en": "The baloma is the spirit of a dead member of the lineage who makes women pregnant while they bathe in the sea, carrying the mother's line forward. Intercourse is seen merely as a way to open the path for the baloma into the womb. The pl article stresses that these beliefs underpin Trobriand matrilineal inheritance.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "baloma-c03",
        "baloma-c04",
        "baloma-c05"
      ]
    }

        "en": "Baloma, spirit of the dead in Trobriand society (Trobriand Islands, Papua New Guinea), studied by Bronislaw Malinowski in the early 20th century, published 1916."

    {

      "quote": "It plays a key role in conception ideologies and explains and maintains the matrilineal descent system by substituting the role of male sperm (\"fathers\") with that of a spirit.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baloma-c04",
      "source_id": "baloma-s1",
      "quote": "The role of sexual intercourse is only indirectly related to conception within Trobriand mentality and seen as a way of opening the path for the baloma to enter the woman's womb.",

    {
      "id": "baloma-c05",
      "source_id": "baloma-s2",
      "quote": "Można zajść w ciążę, kąpiąc się w morzu. Stosunek seksualny ma rolę drugorzędną, stanowi zaledwie sposób otwarcia drogi dla baloma. Wierzenia te stanowią podstawę matrylinearnego systemu dziedziczenia wśród Trobriandczyków.",

      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Naga Bisterne adalah legenda dari New Forest, Inggris, tentang naga yang bersarang di Burley Beacon dan dibunuh seorang ksatria bernama Berkeley.",
    "en": "The Bisterne Dragon is a New Forest legend from England about a dragon that lairs at Burley Beacon and is slain by a knight named Berkeley.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bisterne-dragon-c01",
      "bisterne-dragon-c02",
      "bisterne-dragon-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naga Bisterne adalah legenda dari New Forest di Inggris, terutama berlatar di Bisterne tetapi juga melibatkan Burley dan Lyndhurst. Menurut tradisi setempat, seekor naga bersarang di Burley Beacon, sekitar 3 mil di timur Bisterne, dan setiap pagi \"terbang\" ke Bisterne untuk diberi susu. Seorang ksatria pemberani, biasanya bernama Berkeley, membangun persembunyian dan menunggu bersama dua anjing; anjing-anjing itu menyerang naga, dan sang ksatria menyergapnya, sementara kedua anjing mati dalam pertarungan.",
      "en": "The Bisterne Dragon is a legend of England's New Forest, set mainly in Bisterne but also involving Burley and Lyndhurst. Local tradition says a dragon made its den on Burley Beacon, some 3 miles east of Bisterne, and 'flew' to Bisterne each morning to be given milk. A brave knight, usually called Berkeley, built a hide and waited with two dogs; the dogs set on the dragon and the knight caught it off guard, though both dogs died in the fight.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bisterne-dragon-c01",
        "bisterne-dragon-c02",
        "bisterne-dragon-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Versi lain menyebut ksatria itu menutupi baju zirahnya dengan kaca, dan naga itu mati di luar Lyndhurst lalu tubuhnya menjadi bukit Boltons Bench. Versi tertulis tercatat di pinggir silsilah sebelum 1618 yang disimpan di Berkeley Castle, menyebut pembunuhnya Sir Maurice Berkeley, tuan tanah Bisterne pada abad ke-15; keturunannya memakai lambang naga di depan suar menyala. Lokasi pertarungan masih disebut \"Dragon Fields\". Artikel ja menyebut naga itu juga menuntut daging ternak dan manusia.",
      "en": "Other tellings say the knight covered his armour with glass, and that the dragon died outside Lyndhurst, its body becoming the hill called Boltons Bench. The written version, in the margin of a pedigree roll made before 1618 and kept at Berkeley Castle, names the slayer as Sir Maurice Berkeley, lord of Bisterne manor in the 15th century, whose descendants bore a crest of a dragon before a burning beacon. The supposed battle site is still known as 'Dragon Fields'. The ja article adds that the dragon came to demand the flesh of livestock and people as well.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bisterne-dragon-c04",
        "bisterne-dragon-c05",
        "bisterne-dragon-c06",
        "bisterne-dragon-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "bisterne-dragon-c01",
      "source_id": "bisterne-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The Bisterne Dragon is a legend from the New Forest in England. The story takes place primarily in Bisterne, but also features Burley and Lyndhurst, and is one of the area's most famous pieces of folklore.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga Bisterne (Bisterne Dragon), legenda New Forest, Inggris (England); Bisterne, Burley, Lyndhurst.",
        "en": "Bisterne Dragon, a New Forest legend, England; Bisterne, Burley, Lyndhurst."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bisterne-dragon-c02",
      "source_id": "bisterne-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The local tradition is that a dragon had his den at Burley Beacon, about 3 miles (4.8 km) east of Bisterne, at Burley. There are several versions of the tale, one being that the creature \"flew\" every morning to Bisterne, where it would be supplied with milk.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarang di Burley Beacon, 3 mil timur Bisterne; terbang tiap pagi untuk diberi susu.",
        "en": "Den at Burley Beacon, 3 miles east of Bisterne; flew each morning to be given milk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bisterne-dragon-c03",
      "source_id": "bisterne-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "In order to kill the dragon, a valiant knight (usually named Berkeley) built a hide, and with two dogs lay in wait.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ksatria bernama Berkeley menunggu bersama dua anjing.",
        "en": "A knight named Berkeley lay in wait with two dogs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bisterne-dragon-c04",
      "source_id": "bisterne-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "with the dragon finally dying outside the village of Lyndhurst, his corpse turning into a hill (now known as Boltons Bench)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga mati di luar Lyndhurst, tubuhnya menjadi bukit Boltons Bench; zirah berlapis kaca.",
        "en": "The dragon died outside Lyndhurst, its corpse becoming the hill Boltons Bench; armour covered with glass."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bisterne-dragon-c05",
      "source_id": "bisterne-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The documentary version of this tradition is contained in the margin of a pedigree roll written prior to 1618, and preserved at Berkeley Castle. It actually names the dragon-slayer as Sir Maurice Berkeley, lord of the manor of Bisterne in the 15th century",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Silsilah sebelum 1618 di Berkeley Castle menyebut Sir Maurice Berkeley, tuan tanah Bisterne abad ke-15; lambang naga di depan suar.",
        "en": "A pedigree roll before 1618 at Berkeley Castle names Sir Maurice Berkeley, 15th-century lord of Bisterne; dragon-and-beacon crest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bisterne-dragon-c06",
      "source_id": "bisterne-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The alleged scene of the fight in Bisterne is still called \"Dragon Fields\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lokasi pertarungan disebut Dragon Fields.",
        "en": "The fight site is called Dragon Fields."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bisterne-dragon-c07",
      "source_id": "bisterne-dragon-s2",
      "quote": "バーリー山に棲む竜は、毎日のようにビスターン村に現れては手桶1杯分の牛乳を村人たちに要求していた。やがて竜は家畜の肉や人の肉まで求めるようになった。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel ja (Hampshire dragon): naga menuntut daging ternak dan manusia.",
        "en": "ja article (Hampshire dragon): the dragon demanded the flesh of livestock and people."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Blue Ben adalah naga legenda dari Kilve di Somerset barat, Inggris, yang tinggal di gua serpih di pesisir.",
    "en": "Blue Ben is a legendary dragon from Kilve in west Somerset, England, living in shale caves on the coast.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "blue-ben-c01",
      "blue-ben-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Blue Ben adalah naga legenda dari Kilve di Somerset barat, Inggris. Kisahnya punya beberapa versi; biasanya ia tinggal di gua serpih di pesisir Somerset dan rutin berendam di laut untuk mendinginkan diri setelah menyemburkan api. Agar tidak terjebak di dataran lumpur antara air dan sarangnya, ia membangun jalan batu kapur sebagai jalur aman. Iblis, yang lama mengamatinya, menangkapnya untuk dijadikan tunggangan dan memacunya tanpa ampun melewati api neraka hingga naga itu lolos.",
      "en": "Blue Ben is a legendary dragon of Kilve in western Somerset, England. The tale has several versions; usually he lives in shale caves on the Somerset coast and bathes in the sea to cool off after breathing fire. To avoid getting trapped in the mud flats between sea and lair he built a limestone causeway as a safe path. The Devil, having watched him a while, seized him as a mount and drove him cruelly through hellfire until the dragon broke free.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "blue-ben-c01",
        "blue-ben-c02",
        "blue-ben-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam perjalanan pulang yang tergesa-gesa, Blue Ben melintasi dataran lumpur, terjebak, dan ditelan lumpur. Pada awal abad ke-19, penambangan formasi serpih Blue Lias di luar Kilve menemukan tengkorak fosil Ichthyosaur yang dikatakan sisa Blue Ben; tengkorak itu dipamerkan di Wells and Mendip Museum di Wells atau Museum of Somerset di Taunton.",
      "en": "Rushing home, Blue Ben cut across the mud flats, got stuck, and was swallowed by the mud. In the early 19th century quarrying of the Blue Lias shale outside Kilve exposed a fossil Ichthyosaur skull said to be Blue Ben's remains; it is displayed at the Wells and Mendip Museum in Wells or the Museum of Somerset in Taunton.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "blue-ben-c04",
        "blue-ben-c05",
        "blue-ben-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "blue-ben-c01",
      "source_id": "blue-ben-s1",
      "quote": "Blue Ben is a legendary dragon from Kilve in western Somerset, England. The skull of a fossilized Ichthyosaur on display in a local museum is sometimes pointed out as belonging to him.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Blue Ben, naga legenda dari Kilve, Somerset barat, Inggris (England); tengkorak Ichthyosaur.",
        "en": "Blue Ben, a legendary dragon of Kilve, western Somerset, England; an Ichthyosaur skull."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-ben-c02",
      "source_id": "blue-ben-s1",
      "quote": "Blue Ben typically dwells in the shale caves along the Somerset coast, regularly bathing in the nearby waters to cool himself after breathing fire.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tinggal di gua serpih pesisir Somerset, berendam setelah menyemburkan api.",
        "en": "Dwells in shale caves of the Somerset coast, bathing after breathing fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-ben-c03",
      "source_id": "blue-ben-s1",
      "quote": "The Devil, who had watched Blue Ben for some time, decided to capture him for use as a mount. The Devil rode the dragon mercilessly through the fires of Hell until the dragon escaped.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Iblis (Devil) menungganginya melewati api neraka (Hell); jalan batu kapur.",
        "en": "The Devil rode him through the fires of Hell; limestone causeway."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-ben-c04",
      "source_id": "blue-ben-s1",
      "quote": "Hurrying to get back to the security of his lair, Blue Ben made the mistake of tramping through the mud flats. He got stuck in the mud, which consumed him.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Blue Ben terjebak dan ditelan lumpur.",
        "en": "Blue Ben got stuck and was consumed by the mud."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-ben-c05",
      "source_id": "blue-ben-s1",
      "quote": "In the early 19th century, the Blue Lias shale formation outside of Kilve was quarried, during which process the fossilized skull of an Ichthyosaur was uncovered, that was said to be the remains of Blue Ben.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penambangan Blue Lias awal abad ke-19 menemukan tengkorak Ichthyosaur; Wells and Mendip Museum, Wells; Museum of Somerset, Taunton.",
        "en": "Early 19th-century Blue Lias quarrying uncovered an Ichthyosaur skull; Wells and Mendip Museum, Wells; Museum of Somerset, Taunton."
      }
    },
    {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bogle adalah istilah Northumbria, Cumbria dan Skotlandia untuk hantu atau makhluk folklor yang gemar membingungkan manusia.",
    "en": "Bogle is a Northumbrian, Cumbrian and Scots word for a ghost or folk being that exists to puzzle people.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bogle-c01",
      "bogle-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bogle, boggle atau bogill adalah istilah Northumbria, Cumbria dan Skotlandia untuk hantu atau makhluk folklor, dipakai untuk berbagai makhluk terkait seperti Shellycoat, Barghest, Brag, Hedley Kow, bahkan raksasa. Mereka konon hidup semata untuk membingungkan manusia, bukan untuk benar-benar mencelakai atau melayani. Namanya berasal dari kata Inggris Pertengahan Bugge, asal kata bogey juga.",
      "en": "Bogle, boggle or bogill is a Northumbrian, Cumbrian and Scots word for a ghost or folkloric being, covering related creatures such as the Shellycoat, Barghest, Brag, the Hedley Kow and even giants. Bogles are said to exist simply to baffle people rather than to do them real harm or service. The word comes from Middle English Bugge, the source of bogey as well.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bogle-c01",
        "bogle-c02",
        "bogle-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Gavin Douglas, yang dikutip Robert Burns di awal Tam O' Shanter, menulis \"Of Brownyis and of Bogillis full is this Buke\". Kisah populer Tatty Bogle bercerita tentang bogle yang bersembunyi di ladang kentang untuk menyerang orang atau merusak tanaman, dan digambarkan sebagai orang-orangan sawah. Dalam bahasa Gaelik Skotlandia, padanannya bòcan digambarkan sebagai peri jahat atau hantu.",
      "en": "Gavin Douglas, quoted by Robert Burns at the start of Tam O' Shanter, wrote 'Of Brownyis and of Bogillis full is this Buke'. A popular tale tells of Tatty Bogle, who hid in potato fields to attack careless people or blight the crop and was pictured as a scarecrow. Its Scottish Gaelic counterpart, the bòcan, is described as an evil fairy or ghost.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bogle-c03",
        "bogle-c05",
        "bogle-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "bogle-c01",
      "source_id": "bogle-s1",
      "quote": "is a Northumbrian, Cumbrian and Scots term for a ghost or folkloric being, used for a variety of related folkloric creatures including Shellycoats, Barghests, Brags, the Hedley Kow and even giants",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bogle (boggle, bogill), istilah Northumbria (Northumbrian), Cumbria (Cumbrian) dan Skotlandia (Scots) untuk hantu; Shellycoat, Barghest, Brag, Hedley Kow, raksasa; mirip boggart. Inggris (England) dan Skotlandia (Scotland).",
        "en": "Bogle (boggle, bogill), a Northumbrian, Cumbrian and Scots term for a ghost; Shellycoats, Barghests, Brags, the Hedley Kow, giants; similar to the boggart. England and Scotland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bogle-c02",
      "source_id": "bogle-s1",
      "quote": "They are reputed to live for the simple purpose of perplexing mankind, rather than seriously harming or serving them.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hidup untuk membingungkan manusia.",
        "en": "Live to perplex mankind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bogle-c03",
      "source_id": "bogle-s1",
      "quote": "There is a popular story of a bogle known as Tatty Bogle, who would hide himself in potato fields (hence his name) and either attack unwary humans or cause blight within the patch. This bogle was depicted as a scarecrow",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tatty Bogle bersembunyi di ladang kentang, menyerang manusia atau merusak tanaman; orang-orangan sawah.",
        "en": "Tatty Bogle hid in potato fields, attacking people or blighting crops; a scarecrow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bogle-c04",
      "source_id": "bogle-s1",
      "quote": "The name is derived from the Middle-English Bugge (from which the term bogey is also derived)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dari kata Inggris Pertengahan (Middle-English) Bugge; asal kata bogey.",
        "en": "From Middle English Bugge; also the source of bogey."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bogle-c05",
      "source_id": "bogle-s1",
      "quote": "One of the most famous usages of the term was by Gavin Douglas, who was in turn quoted by Robert Burns at the beginning of Tam O' Shanter: Of Brownyis and of Bogillis full is this Buke.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gavin Douglas, dikutip Robert Burns dalam Tam O' Shanter.",
        "en": "Gavin Douglas, quoted by Robert Burns in Tam O' Shanter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bogle-c06",
      "source_id": "bogle-s2",
      "quote": "Tha bòcan na dheamhain. 'S e Sìthiche olc neo taibhse.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bòcan (Gaelik Skotlandia, Scottish Gaelic): setan, peri jahat atau hantu.",
        "en": "Bòcan (Scottish Gaelic): a demon, evil fairy or ghost."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Bownessie adalah julukan untuk monster danau yang diduga hidup di Windermere, Cumbria, Inggris, mirip monster Loch Ness.",
    "en": "Bownessie is the nickname for a lake monster supposedly living in Windermere, Cumbria, England, akin to the Loch Ness monster.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bownessie-c01",
      "bownessie-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sebagian orang percaya ada monster danau di Windermere, danau alami terbesar di Inggris di Cumbria, mirip monster yang konon hidup di Loch Ness. Pada 2011 diambil foto-foto aneh dari makhluk yang diduga itu, dan ia dijuluki dengan sayang \"Bownessie\". Artikel fr menyebut namanya, juga Bow Nessie, merupakan penghormatan bagi \"sepupunya\" Nessie, dan makhluk ini digambarkan mirip ular laut atau plesiosaurus.",
      "en": "Some believe a lake monster lives in Windermere, England's largest natural lake, in Cumbria, much like the creature said to inhabit Loch Ness. Odd photographs of the supposed animal were taken in 2011, and it was fondly dubbed 'Bownessie'. The fr article says the name, also written Bow Nessie, honours its 'cousin' Nessie, and that it is described as looking like a sea serpent or a plesiosaur.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bownessie-c01",
        "bownessie-c02",
        "bownessie-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Setelah klaim baru para wisatawan pada Februari 2011, Dr Ian Winfield dari pusat ekologi dan hidrologi Universitas Lancaster menyatakan tidak ada ikan sebesar yang digambarkan yang dapat hidup di Windermere, dan danau itu dipindai setiap bulan tanpa menemukan apa pun. Menurut The Telegraph, foto baru itu cocok dengan gambaran Steve Burnip pada 2006; hingga kini sembilan orang mengaku pernah melihat Bownessie.",
      "en": "After fresh reports from tourists in February 2011, Dr Ian Winfield of the Lancaster University ecology and hydrology centre said no fish as large as those described could live in Windermere, and that the lake is surveyed monthly with nothing like it ever found. According to The Telegraph the new photograph matched the account Steve Burnip gave in 2006, and nine people in all have claimed to see Bownessie.",

      ]

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bugbear adalah makhluk legenda atau sejenis hobgoblin, mirip boogeyman, yang dulu dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak yang tidak patuh.",
    "en": "The bugbear is a legendary creature or kind of hobgoblin, like the boogeyman, once used to scare disobedient children.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bugbear-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bugbear adalah makhluk legenda atau sejenis hobgoblin yang sebanding dengan boogeyman (bugaboo, babau, cucuy) dan makhluk folklor lain yang dulu dipakai di sejumlah budaya untuk menakuti anak nakal. Namanya berasal dari kata Inggris Pertengahan \"bugge\" (sesuatu yang menakutkan), atau mungkin kata Welsh Kuno bwg (roh jahat) atau Skots Kuno bogill (goblin). Di Inggris abad pertengahan, bugbear digambarkan sebagai beruang menyeramkan yang mengintai di hutan untuk menakuti anak-anak.",
      "en": "A bugbear is a legendary creature or sort of hobgoblin akin to the boogeyman (bugaboo, babau, cucuy) and other folk beings once used in various cultures to frighten naughty children. The name comes from Middle English 'bugge', a frightening thing, or perhaps from Old Welsh bwg, 'evil spirit', or Old Scots bogill, 'goblin'. In medieval England the bugbear was imagined as a sinister bear lurking in the woods to frighten children.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bugbear-c01",
        "bugbear-c02",
        "bugbear-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Gambaran ini muncul dalam The Buggbears, saduran dari La Spiritata karya Antonio Francesco Grazzini (1561). Artikel ja menyebutnya peri berwujud manusia berbulu lebat, sejenis goblin, yang memakan anak nakal; para ibu menakuti anak-anak bahwa mereka akan dimakan bugbear. Dalam fantasi modern dan permainan peran, bugbear biasanya lawan kecil berupa goblinoid besar berbulu.",
      "en": "This image appears in The Buggbears, adapted from Antonio Francesco Grazzini's La Spiritata (1561). The ja article describes it as a goblin-like fairy in the form of a shaggy person that eats bad children, with mothers warning that the bugbear will eat them. In modern fantasy and role-playing games bugbears are usually minor foes, large hairy goblinoids.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bugbear-c03",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "bugbear-c01",
      "source_id": "bugbear-s1",
      "quote": "A bugbear is a legendary creature or type of hobgoblin comparable to the boogeyman (or bugaboo or babau or cucuy), and other creatures of folklore, all of which were historically used in some cultures to frighten disobedient children.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bugbear, sejenis hobgoblin, mirip boogeyman (bugaboo, babau, cucuy); menakuti anak tidak patuh.",
        "en": "Bugbear, a type of hobgoblin, like the boogeyman (bugaboo, babau, cucuy); frightens disobedient children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bugbear-c02",
      "source_id": "bugbear-s1",
      "quote": "In medieval England, the bugbear was depicted as a creepy bear that lurked in the woods to scare children.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Inggris (England) abad pertengahan: beruang seram di hutan; nama dari Inggris Pertengahan bugge, Welsh Kuno bwg, Skots Kuno bogill.",
        "en": "In medieval England: a creepy bear in the woods; name from Middle English bugge, Old Welsh bwg, Old Scots bogill."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bugbear-c03",
      "source_id": "bugbear-s1",
      "quote": "It was described in this manner in The Buggbears, an adaptation, with additions, from Antonio Francesco Grazzini’s La Spiritata (‘The Possessed [Woman]’, 1561).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "The Buggbears, saduran La Spiritata karya Antonio Francesco Grazzini (1561).",
        "en": "The Buggbears, adapted from Antonio Francesco Grazzini's La Spiritata (1561)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bugbear-c04",
      "source_id": "bugbear-s1",
      "quote": "They also appear as monsters, described as large, hairy goblinoids, in the canon of popular fantasy role-playing games.",

      "statement": {

        "en": "Large hairy goblinoid monsters in fantasy role-playing games."

    },
    {

      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "bugbear-c06",
      "source_id": "bugbear-s2",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Caoineag (\"si peratap\") adalah roh perempuan dalam folklor Skotlandia, sejenis banshee Dataran Tinggi yang meratapi kematian anggota klannya.",
    "en": "The caoineag ('weeper') is a female spirit of Scottish folklore, a Highland banshee who keens for coming deaths in her clan.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "caoineag-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Caoineag adalah roh perempuan dalam folklor Skotlandia dan sejenis banshee Dataran Tinggi; namanya berarti \"si peratap\". Biasanya ia tak terlihat dan meramalkan kematian dalam klannya dengan meratap di malam hari di air terjun, sungai, loch, lembah atau lereng gunung. Berbeda dengan bean nighe, ia tidak dapat didekati, ditanyai, atau dipaksa mengabulkan permintaan. Nama setempat lainnya antara lain caointeag, caoineachag, caointeachag dan caoidheag.",
      "en": "The caoineag is a female spirit of Scottish folklore and a kind of Highland banshee, her name meaning 'weeper'. Usually unseen, she foretells death in her clan by keening at night beside a waterfall, stream or loch, in a glen or on a hillside. Unlike the related bean nighe she cannot be approached, questioned or made to grant wishes. Local variants of her name include caointeag, caoineachag, caointeachag and caoidheag.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "caoineag-c01",
        "caoineag-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Folkloris Alexander Carmichael dalam Carmina Gadelica menyebut ia meramalkan kematian orang yang gugur dalam perang. Sebelum Pembantaian Glencoe, caoineag Klan MacDonald terdengar meratap malam demi malam; mereka yang ketakutan meninggalkan lembah dan selamat. Versi lain, caointeach di Islay, muncul berselendang hijau di depan rumah orang sakit dan meratap di pintu. Artikel gd mencatat bahwa bila ditanya \"Siapa itu?\" ia menjawab \"Siapa lagi kalau bukan Caoineag kecil yang berduka!\"",
      "en": "The folklorist Alexander Carmichael, in Carmina Gadelica, says she foretells the deaths of men killed in battle. Night after night ahead of the Massacre of Glencoe, Clan MacDonald's caoineag was heard crying, and the people who took her warning fled the glen and lived. Another form, the caointeach of Islay, appears in a green shawl outside a sick person's house and laments at the door. The gd article records that when asked 'Who is there?' she replies, 'Who but little Caoineag of the sorrow!'",
      "claim_ids": [
        "caoineag-c03",
        "caoineag-c04",
        "caoineag-c05",
        "caoineag-c06"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Coblyn adalah makhluk mitos mirip gnome yang konon menghuni tambang dan galian batu di Wales serta permukiman Welsh di Amerika.",
    "en": "The coblyn is a mythical gnome-like being said to haunt the mines and quarries of Wales and Welsh settlements in America.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Coblyn (juga koblyn, jamak coblynau atau coblynod) adalah makhluk mitos mirip gnome yang konon menghuni tambang dan galian batu di Wales serta daerah permukiman Welsh di Amerika. Dalam British Goblins, Wirt Sikes menggambarkan coblynau sebagai peri ramah yang pendek, sangat jelek, sering berpakaian penambang dan membawa perkakas untuk meniru kerja para penambang dengan semangat tetapi sia-sia.",
      "en": "The coblyn (also koblyn, plural coblynau or coblynod) is a mythical being rather like a gnome, believed to frequent mines and quarries in Wales and in places in America settled by the Welsh. In British Goblins Wirt Sikes depicts coblynau as friendly fairies, short and very ugly, often in miners' dress and carrying tools with which they copy the miners' labour eagerly but to no effect.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "coblyn-c01",
        "coblyn-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seperti Knocker dalam folklor Cornwall, mereka menuntun penambang ke urat bijih terkaya dengan bunyi ketukan; bila tidak dihormati mereka melempar batu yang biasanya tidak berbahaya, dan penambang menganggap mereka pembawa untung. Satu kisah menyebut anak-anak melihat lima belas atau enam belas coblynau berpakaian merah menari Morris di sebuah ladang di Bodfari. Kata Coblyn berkerabat dengan goblin dan tercatat dalam kamus bahasa Welsh dan Inggris karya William Salesbury tahun 1547.",
      "en": "Like the Knockers of Cornish lore, they guide miners to the richest veins of ore with their knocking, throw mostly harmless stones when slighted, and are counted good luck by miners. One account has children watching fifteen or sixteen red-clad coblynau Morris dancing in a field at Bodfari. The word Coblyn is akin to goblin and is listed in William Salesbury's 1547 Welsh-English dictionary.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "coblyn-c03",
        "coblyn-c04",
        "coblyn-c05",
        "coblyn-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "coblyn-c01",
      "source_id": "coblyn-s1",
      "quote": "is a mythical gnome-like creature that is said to haunt the mines and quarries of Wales and areas of Welsh settlements in America.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Coblyn (koblyn, coblynau, coblynod), mirip gnome, menghuni tambang di Wales dan permukiman Welsh di Amerika (America).",
        "en": "Coblyn (koblyn, coblynau, coblynod), gnome-like, haunts mines of Wales and Welsh settlements in America."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coblyn-c02",
      "source_id": "coblyn-s1",
      "quote": "In British Goblins, Wirt Sikes describes coblynau as friendly fairies, short (between half a yard tall and just shorter than humans), very ugly, and often dressed in miner's clothing",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wirt Sikes (British Goblins): peri ramah, pendek, sangat jelek, berpakaian penambang.",
        "en": "Wirt Sikes (British Goblins): friendly fairies, short, very ugly, in miners' clothing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coblyn-c03",
      "source_id": "coblyn-s1",
      "quote": "Like the Knockers of Cornish folklore, they often help miners to the richest veins of ore or other treasures by their peculiar knocking sound. They may throw rocks at miners if disrespected, but the rocks are typically harmless. Miners generally consider coblynau to be good luck.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seperti Knocker Cornwall (Cornish), menuntun ke urat bijih; lempar batu; pembawa untung.",
        "en": "Like Cornish Knockers, lead to ore veins; throw rocks; good luck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coblyn-c04",
      "source_id": "coblyn-s1",
      "quote": "One account tells of a group of children seeing a group of fifteen or sixteen coblynau, dressed in all red and wearing yellow-spotted red handkerchiefs around their heads, Morris dancing in a Bodfari field.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lima belas atau enam belas coblynau berpakaian merah menari Morris di ladang Bodfari.",
        "en": "Fifteen or sixteen coblynau in red Morris dancing in a Bodfari field."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coblyn-c05",
      "source_id": "coblyn-s1",
      "quote": "The word Coblyn is related to the English word Goblin, and may derive from a Germanic source akin to the German Kobold, via the French Gobelin. It appears in William Salesbury's 1547 Welsh-English dictionary as koblyn",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Coblyn berkerabat dengan Goblin (goblin), Kobold, Gobelin; kamus William Salesbury 1547. Kamus bahasa Welsh dan Inggris (English).",
        "en": "Coblyn related to Goblin, Kobold, Gobelin; William Salesbury's 1547 dictionary."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coblyn-c06",
      "source_id": "coblyn-s2",
      "quote": "De Coblynau is verwant met de Knockers uit Cornwall, het wezen heeft overeenkomsten met de Blue Cap en het Kaolmenke uit de Lage Landen en de Wichtel uit Zuid-Duitsland.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel nl: berkerabat dengan Knockers Cornwall, Blue Cap, Kaolmenke, Wichtel; menari di Bodfari.",
        "en": "nl article: related to the Cornish Knockers, Blue Cap, Kaolmenke, Wichtel; dancing at Bodfari."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "El Sombrerón adalah tokoh legenda paling terkenal di Guatemala, lelaki bertopi besar yang mengepang rambut kuda dan perempuan muda serta menyanyikan serenade.",
    "en": "El Sombrerón is one of Guatemala's best-known legendary figures, a man in a huge hat who braids horses' and young women's hair and sings serenades.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "el-sombreron-c01",
      "el-sombreron-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "El Sombrerón adalah tokoh legenda dan salah satu legenda paling terkenal di Guatemala, juga sosok penakut anak di Meksiko; ia dikenal pula sebagai Tzipitio atau Tzizimite. Ia digambarkan sebagai lelaki berpakaian hitam dengan sabuk tebal mengilap, topi hitam besar, dan sepatu bot yang berisik. Ia suka menunggang kuda dan mengepang ekor serta surainya, atau bulu anjing bila tak ada kuda. Ia mengejar gadis berambut panjang dan bermata besar, mengepang rambutnya dan bernyanyi dengan gitar peraknya, tetapi menaruh tanah di piringnya hingga gadis itu tak bisa makan atau tidur.",
      "en": "El Sombrerón is a legendary figure and among the most famous legends of Guatemala, and a bogeyman in Mexico as well; he also goes by Tzipitio or Tzizimite. He is pictured as a man in black with a thick shining belt, a great black hat and noisy boots. He loves riding horses and plaiting their tails and manes, or dogs' hair when no horse is at hand. He pursues girls with long hair and big eyes, braiding their hair and playing his silver guitar for them, yet he puts earth in the girl's food so she can neither eat nor sleep.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "el-sombreron-c01",
        "el-sombreron-c02",
        "el-sombreron-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia muncul saat senja sambil menggiring kawanan bagal pengangkut arang. Dalam kisahnya, gadis bernama Susana di lingkungan La Recolección tak bisa tidur atau makan karenanya, sampai orang tuanya memotong rambutnya dan membawanya ke gereja untuk diberkati. Miguel Ángel Asturias menulis \"Legend of El Sombrerón\" dalam Legends of Guatemala (1930), dan pada 1950 ia menjadi subjek film. Artikel es menggambarkannya sebagai kurcaci bertopi besar yang mengepang rambut perempuan di malam hari.",
      "en": "He shows up at dusk driving a string of mules loaded with coal. In the tale, a girl named Susana in La Recolección could neither sleep nor eat because of him until her parents cut her hair and took it to church to be blessed. Miguel Ángel Asturias wrote 'Legend of El Sombrerón' in his 1930 Legends of Guatemala, and in 1950 he was the subject of a film. The es article describes him as a dwarf in an enormous hat who braids women's hair at night.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "el-sombreron-c04",
        "el-sombreron-c05",
        "el-sombreron-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "el-sombreron-c01",
      "source_id": "el-sombreron-s1",
      "quote": "This character is also known with other names, like Tzipitio, the goblin, and sometimes Tzizimite, his main characteristics are always the same: a tall man with black dress, a thick and brilliant belt; he wears a black, large hat, and boots that make a lot of noise when he walks.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "El Sombrerón, legenda Guatemala dan sosok penakut di Meksiko (Mexico); Tzipitio, Tzizimite; lelaki berpakaian hitam, sabuk mengilap, topi hitam besar, bot berisik.",
        "en": "El Sombrerón, legend of Guatemala and bogeyman in Mexico; Tzipitio, Tzizimite; man in black, shining belt, large black hat, noisy boots."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-sombreron-c02",
      "source_id": "el-sombreron-s1",
      "quote": "He likes to ride horses and braid their tails and manes. When he cannot find horses, he braids the hair of dogs. He also likes to court young ladies who have long hair and big eyes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mengepang ekor dan surai kuda, bulu anjing; mendekati gadis berambut panjang.",
        "en": "Braids horses' tails and manes, dogs' hair; courts long-haired girls."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-sombreron-c03",
      "source_id": "el-sombreron-s1",
      "quote": "When he likes one in particular, he follows her, braids her hair, and serenades to her with his silver guitar; but he also puts soil in her plate and she is not able to eat or sleep.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mengepang rambut gadis, serenade dengan gitar perak, menaruh tanah di piringnya; gadis tak bisa makan atau tidur.",
        "en": "Braids the girl's hair, serenades with a silver guitar, puts soil in her plate; she cannot eat or sleep."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-sombreron-c04",
      "source_id": "el-sombreron-s1",
      "quote": "El Sombrerón appears at dusk, dragging along a group of mules carrying coal, with whom he travels around the city and its neighborhoods.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Muncul senja dengan bagal pengangkut arang; La Recolección; bersama La Llorona, Cadejo, Siguanaba legenda penting Guatemala.",
        "en": "Appears at dusk with coal-laden mules; La Recolección; with La Llorona, Cadejo, Siguanaba among Guatemala's key legends."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-sombreron-c05",
      "source_id": "el-sombreron-s1",
      "quote": "\"Legend of El Sombrerón\" is the title of one of the short stories in Guatemalan Nobel-prize winner Miguel Ángel Asturias' 1930 collection Legends of Guatemala. In 1950, El Sombrerón became the subject of an eponymous film, one of the first films shot in Guatemala.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "el-sombreron-c06",
      "source_id": "el-sombreron-s2",
      "quote": "El Sombrerón, también llamado Tzitzimite, es un personaje mitológico de una de las más conocidas  Leyendas de Guatemala, reflejado en libros y en el cine. Está representado como un enano que lleva consigo un enorme sombrero, dando serenatas a las mujeres que elige.",

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Eloko (jamak: Biloko) adalah makhluk mirip kurcaci dalam bahasa Mongo-Nkundo yang tinggal di hutan dan diyakini roh leluhur.",
    "en": "Eloko (plural Biloko) is a Mongo-Nkundo word for a dwarf-like forest being believed to be an ancestral spirit.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "eloko-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Eloko (jamak Biloko) adalah istilah dalam bahasa Mongo-Nkundo untuk sejenis makhluk mirip kurcaci yang tinggal di hutan, dan diyakini sebagai roh leluhur penduduk setempat. Biloko menghuni bagian hutan hujan paling lebat dan gelap di Zaïre tengah, menjaga dengan cemburu dan ganas harta mereka: hewan buruan dan buah-buahan langka. Mereka menghantui hutan karena punya dendam terhadap orang hidup dan umumnya sangat kejam.",
      "en": "Eloko (plural Biloko) is a Mongo-Nkundo word for a sort of dwarf-like creature dwelling in the forests, held to be the spirits of the local people's ancestors. Biloko occupy the thickest, darkest stretches of rainforest in central Zaïre, guarding their wealth, the game and rare fruits of the forest, with jealous ferocity. They haunt the forest because of a grudge against the living and are generally very cruel.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "eloko-c01",
        "eloko-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Biloko tinggal di pohon berongga dan hanya berpakaian daun; mereka tak berambut, hanya rumput tumbuh di tubuhnya, bermata tajam, bermoncong dengan mulut yang dapat terbuka cukup lebar untuk menelan tubuh manusia, dan bercakar panjang. Lonceng kecil mereka diyakini dapat menyihir orang yang lewat, dan jimat dapat melindungi dari sihir itu. Dalam kisah yang dikutip ahli bahasa Bantu Jan Knappert, istri seorang pemburu yang terpikat bunyi lonceng membukakan pintu bagi eloko, yang hanya makan daging manusia; malamnya sang suami hanya menemukan tulang-tulangnya.",
      "en": "Biloko live in hollow trees, wear only leaves and are hairless, with grass growing on their bodies, piercing eyes, snouts whose mouths open wide enough to take in a human body, and long sharp claws. Their little bells are believed to enchant passers-by, and an amulet can guard against this. In a tale cited by the Bantu linguist Jan Knappert, a hunter's wife, charmed by the bell, opened the door to an eloko that ate only human flesh; that night her husband found just her bones.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "eloko-c03",
        "eloko-c04",
        "eloko-c05",
        "eloko-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Failinis (atau Ṡalinnis/Shalinnis) adalah anjing dalam Siklus Mitologis sastra Irlandia, milik Lugh Lámhfhada dari Tuatha Dé Danann; ia salah satu barang eric (tebusan) yang dituntut dari putra-putra Tuireann. Asalnya anak anjing milik pandai besi atau raja Iruaith. Dalam Lebor Gabála Érenn abad ke-12 ia hanya disebut \"anak anjing pandai besi kerajaan Ioruath\", dan baru dinamai Failinis dalam roman Oidheadh Chloinne Tuireann. Eugene O'Curry menduga nama itu pembalikan dari \"Inis Fáil\", nama kuno Irlandia.",
      "en": "In the Mythological Cycle of Irish literature, Failinis (or Ṡalinnis/Shalinnis) is the hound of Lugh Lámhfhada of the Tuatha Dé Danann, one of the eric, or reparation, items demanded of the sons of Tuireann. It began as the whelp of the smith or the king of Iruaith. The 12th-century Lebor Gabála Érenn calls it only the 'whelp of the royal smith of Ioruath'; the name Failinis appears in the later romance Oidheadh Chloinne Tuireann. Eugene O'Curry thought the name a reversal of 'Inis Fáil', an old name for Ireland.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "failinis-c02",

Kemudian, dalam Siklus Fenian, anjing Lugh (dengan nama Fer Mac) dimiliki tiga orang asing dari Iruaith yang datang ke Irlandia dan bertemu Fíanna pimpinan Fionn mac Cumhaill.       "en": "The hound could not be beaten in battle, caught every wild beast it met, and by magic turned any running water it bathed in into wine.   "short_description": {
    "id": "Fetch adalah kembaran gaib atau penampakan orang yang masih hidup dalam folklor Irlandia, yang dianggap pertanda kematian.",
    "en": "A fetch is a supernatural double or apparition of a living person in Irish folklore, taken as an omen of death.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fetch-folklore-c01",
      "fetch-folklore-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fetch adalah kembaran gaib atau penampakan dari orang yang masih hidup. Melihat fetch dianggap pertanda pasti, biasanya kematian yang akan datang bagi diri sendiri atau orang tercinta. Fetch digambarkan sebagai kembaran hantu yang persis sama; namun menurut John dan Michael Banim, bila kembaran itu muncul pagi hari dan bukan malam, itu pertanda umur panjang. Ia mirip doppelgänger Jermanik dan beberapa konsep wraith Inggris. Francis Grose mengaitkan istilah itu dengan Inggris Utara dalam Provincial Glossary (1787).",
      "en": "A fetch is a supernatural double or apparition of a living person, and seeing one is taken as a sure omen, usually of coming death for the viewer or someone dear. It is an exact ghostly twin; yet John and Michael Banim say that a double appearing in the morning rather than evening foretells long life. It resembles the Germanic doppelgänger and some ideas of the British wraith. Francis Grose tied the term to Northern England in his 1787 Provincial Glossary.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fetch-folklore-c01",
        "fetch-folklore-c02",
        "fetch-folklore-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Etimologinya kabur. Mungkin dari kata kerja \"fetch\" (menjemput): gabungan \"fetch-life\", merujuk psikopompos yang menjemput jiwa orang sekarat, tercatat dalam terjemahan Aeneid oleh Richard Stanyhurst tahun 1583. Fetch muncul dalam sastra Irlandia sejak awal abad ke-19, misalnya kisah gotik \"The Fetches\" karya John dan Michael Banim dalam Tales by the O'Hara Family (1825).",
      "en": "The origin of the word is obscure. It may come from the verb 'fetch': the compound 'fetch-life', for a psychopomp who 'fetches' dying souls, is found in Richard Stanyhurst's 1583 translation of the Aeneid. The fetch entered Irish literature from the early 19th century, as in John and Michael Banim's Gothic tale 'The Fetches' in Tales by the O'Hara Family (1825).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fetch-folklore-c04",
        "fetch-folklore-c05"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Finnbhennach adalah banteng istimewa milik Ailill mac Máta, raja Connacht, dalam mitologi Irlandia; namanya berarti \"bertanduk putih\".",
    "en": "Finnbhennach is a remarkable bull owned by Ailill, king of Connacht, in Irish mythology; the name means 'white-horned'.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "finnbhennach-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Irlandia, Finnbhennach (juga Findbennach) adalah banteng istimewa milik Ailill mac Mágach, raja Connacht; namanya berarti \"bertanduk putih\". Bersama banteng Donn Cuailnge ia tokoh sentral dalam kisah terkenal Táin Bó Cuailnge. Kelahirannya diceritakan dalam De chophur in da muccida: dua penggembala babi raja-raja peri Connacht dan Munster beradu sihir, berubah wujud berkali-kali hingga menjadi cacing air yang ditelan dua sapi, lalu lahir sebagai Finnbhennach dan Donn Cuailnge. Artikel ga menyebut salah satunya bernama Rucht, penggembala babi Ochall Ochne.",
      "en": "In Irish mythology Finnbhennach (also Findbennach) is a remarkable bull belonging to Ailill, king of Connacht; the name means 'white-horned'. With the bull Donn Cuailnge it is central to the famous Táin Bó Cuailnge. Its birth is told in De chophur in da muccida: two swineherds of the fairy kings of Connacht and Munster duel in magic through a run of shapes until, as water worms, they are swallowed by two cows and reborn as Finnbhennach and Donn Cuailnge. The ga article names one of them Rucht, swineherd of Ochall Ochne.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "finnbhennach-c02",
        "finnbhennach-c03",
        "finnbhennach-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Banteng itu dulu milik Medb, ratu Connacht, tetapi karena tak sudi dimiliki perempuan, ia pindah ke kawanan Ailill. Agar setara dengan suaminya, Medb mengerahkan pasukan untuk merampas Donn Cuailnge dari Cooley di Ulster. Setelah Donn Cuailnge dibawa ke Connacht, kedua banteng bertarung; Finnbhennach terbunuh dan Donn Cuailnge terluka parah hingga mati.",
      "en": "The bull had once been Medb's, queen of Connacht, but scorning to be owned by a woman it joined Ailill's herd. To match her husband Medb gathered an army to seize Donn Cuailnge from Cooley in Ulster. Once Donn Cuailnge was brought to Connacht the two bulls fought; Finnbhennach was killed and Donn Cuailnge was fatally hurt.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "finnbhennach-c01",
        "finnbhennach-c04",
        "finnbhennach-c06"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "finnbhennach-c01",
      "source_id": "finnbhennach-s1",
      "quote": "The longest and most important story of the cycle is the Táin Bó Cúailnge or \"Cattle Raid of Cooley\", in which Medb raises an enormous army to invade the Cooley peninsula and steal the Ulaid's prize bull, Donn Cúailnge",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Táin Bó Cúailnge: Medb, ratu Connacht, merampas banteng Donn Cúailnge (Donn Cuailnge) dari Cooley, Ulster; Ailill mac Máta.",
        "en": "Táin Bó Cúailnge: Medb, queen of Connacht, seizes the bull Donn Cúailnge (Donn Cuailnge) from Cooley, Ulster; Ailill mac Máta."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "finnbhennach-c02",
      "source_id": "finnbhennach-s2",
      "quote": "Ym mytholeg Iwerddon, tarw arbennig sy'n perthyn i Ailill mac Mágach, brenin Connacht, yw Finnbhennach (mae'r amrywiadau ar sillafiad yr enw yn cynnwys Findbennach). Ystyr ei enw yw \"Gwyn-gorniog\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish, Iwerddon), Finnbhennach (Findbennach), banteng milik Ailill (Ailill mac Mágach), raja Connacht; berarti bertanduk putih (Gwyn-gorniog); bersama Donn Cuailnge dalam Táin Bó Cuailnge.",
        "en": "In Irish mythology, Finnbhennach (Findbennach), bull of Ailill (Ailill mac Mágach), king of Connacht; means white-horned (Gwyn-gorniog); with Donn Cuailnge in the Táin Bó Cuailnge."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "finnbhennach-c03",
      "source_id": "finnbhennach-s2",
      "quote": "Ceir hanes geni Finnbhennach yn y chwedl De chophur in da muccida, hanes dau feichiad (bugail moch) brenhinoedd Tylwyth Teg taleithiau Connacht a Munster.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kelahiran dalam De chophur in da muccida: dua penggembala babi raja peri Connacht dan Munster.",
        "en": "Birth in De chophur in da muccida: two swineherds of the fairy kings of Connacht and Munster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "finnbhennach-c04",
      "source_id": "finnbhennach-s2",
      "quote": "Ar un adeg roedd y tarw yn perthyn i Medb, ond gan ei fod yn anfodlon cael gwraig yn berchennog arno, crwydrodd i ffwrdd ac ymuno â buches Ailill.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dulu milik Medb, pindah ke kawanan Ailill; dibunuh Donn Cuailnge di Connacht.",
        "en": "Once Medb's, moved to Ailill's herd; killed by Donn Cuailnge in Connacht."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "finnbhennach-c05",
      "source_id": "finnbhennach-s3",
      "quote": "Sa Rúraíocht i Miotaseolaíocht na nGael, tarbh ghraíre olltorthúil ab ea Fionnbheannach (Sean-Ghaeilge Finnbennach)  le rí Ailill na gConnacht.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel ga: Fionnbheannach (Finnbennach), banteng milik raja Ailill dari Connacht; Rucht, penggembala babi Ochall Ochne.",
        "en": "ga article: Fionnbheannach (Finnbennach), bull of king Ailill of Connacht; Rucht, swineherd of Ochall Ochne."

      "source_id": "finnbhennach-s3",
      "quote": "Throid an dá tharbh; maraíodh Fionnbheannach agus fuair Donn Cuailnge goin a bháis.",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Kedua banteng bertarung; Fionnbheannach terbunuh, Donn Cuailnge terluka parah.",
        "en": "The two bulls fought; Fionnbheannach was killed, Donn Cuailnge mortally wounded."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ghillie Dhu (Gille Dubh) adalah peri lelaki penyendiri dalam folklor Skotlandia, berambut gelap dan berpakaian daun serta lumut, penjaga hutan birch.",
    "en": "The Ghillie Dhu (Gille Dubh) is a solitary male fairy of Scottish folklore, dark-haired and clad in leaves and moss, guardian of a birch wood.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ghillie-dhu-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ghillie Dhu atau Gille Dubh adalah peri lelaki penyendiri dalam folklor Skotlandia. Ia berambut gelap dan berpakaian daun serta lumut, yang menjadi asal nama ghillie suit. Ia terutama muncul dalam kisah dari akhir abad ke-18, tinggal di hutan birch di Dataran Tinggi barat laut Skotlandia, menjaga hutan dengan garang dari orang luar tetapi baik kepada anak-anak. Ghillie adalah padanan Inggris untuk kata Gaelik Skotlandia gille (\"pemuda\"), dan dubh berarti \"gelap\".",
      "en": "The Ghillie Dhu or Gille Dubh is a solitary male fairy of Scottish folklore. He was dark-haired and dressed in leaves and moss, which gave the ghillie suit its name. He features mostly in accounts from the late 18th century, inhabiting a birch wood in the north-west Highlands of Scotland, guarding the forest fiercely from outsiders yet treating children kindly. Ghillie renders the Scottish Gaelic gille, 'lad', and dubh means 'dark'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ghillie-dhu-c01",
        "ghillie-dhu-c02",
        "ghillie-dhu-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Katharine Briggs ia roh gunung yang lembut, pemalu tetapi bisa \"liar\", bertubuh kecil. Ia tinggal di hutan birch dekat Loch an Draing di Gairloch. Kisah paling terkenal menyebut gadis bernama Jessie Macrae yang tersesat di hutan; Ghillie Dhu menghiburnya dan mengantarnya pulang. Tuan tanah Sir Hector Mackenzie dari Gairloch kemudian mengajak lima bangsawan Mackenzie memburunya, tetapi gagal; menurut Patricia Monaghan, ia tak pernah terlihat lagi.",
      "en": "Katharine Briggs calls him a gentle mountain spirit, shy but able to be 'wild', and small in build. He lived in the birch woods by Loch an Draing in Gairloch. The best-known story tells of Jessie Macrae, a girl lost in the woods whom the Ghillie Dhu comforted and guided home. The landowner Sir Hector Mackenzie of Gairloch later had five Mackenzie gentlemen hunt him, without success; according to Patricia Monaghan he was never seen again.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ghillie-dhu-c03",
        "ghillie-dhu-c04",
        "ghillie-dhu-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "ghillie-dhu-c01",

      "quote": "is a solitary male fairy described in Scottish folklore. He was said to be dark-haired, and clothed in leaves and moss, from which the ghillie suit got its name. He appears primarily in accounts from the late 18th century, living in a birch wood in the north-west Highlands of Scotland.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ghillie Dhu (Gille Dubh), peri lelaki penyendiri folklor Skotlandia (Scottish, Scotland), rambut gelap, berpakaian daun dan lumut, asal nama ghillie suit; akhir abad ke-18; hutan birch Dataran Tinggi (Highlands) barat laut; menjaga hutan, baik kepada anak.",
        "en": "Ghillie Dhu (Gille Dubh), a solitary male fairy of Scottish folklore, dark-haired, clothed in leaves and moss, origin of the ghillie suit; late 18th century; birch wood in the north-west Highlands; guards the forest, kind to children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ghillie-dhu-c02",
      "source_id": "ghillie-dhu-s1",
      "quote": "Ghillie is an English equivalent of the Scottish Gaelic word gille . English lexicographer Edward Dwelly lists gille as a \"lad\", \"youth\", or \"boy\"; with dubh meaning \"dark\" or \"dark-haired\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ghillie padanan Inggris (English) untuk kata Gaelik Skotlandia (Scottish Gaelic) gille (pemuda); dubh berarti gelap.",
        "en": "Ghillie, English equivalent of Scottish Gaelic gille (lad); dubh means dark."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ghillie-dhu-c03",
      "source_id": "ghillie-dhu-s1",
      "quote": "He was said to live in the birch woods near Loch an Draing, in the north-west Highland area of Gairloch.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hutan birch dekat Loch an Draing, Gairloch; Katharine Briggs: roh gunung lembut, pemalu, liar, kecil.",
        "en": "Birch woods near Loch an Draing, Gairloch; Katharine Briggs: gentle mountain spirit, timid, wild, small."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ghillie-dhu-c04",
      "source_id": "ghillie-dhu-s1",
      "quote": "The best known account of the Ghillie Dhu involves a girl named Jessie Macrae, who lived near the woods. She wandered into the woods and became lost as the sun went down.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jessie Macrae tersesat; Ghillie Dhu menghibur dan mengantarnya pulang.",
        "en": "Jessie Macrae got lost; the Ghillie Dhu comforted her and led her home."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ghillie-dhu-c05",
      "source_id": "ghillie-dhu-s1",
      "quote": "At some point, landowner Sir Hector Mackenzie of Gairloch invited a group of five Mackenzie dignitaries to hunt and capture the Ghillie Dhu, which he believed posed a threat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sir Hector Mackenzie dari (of) Gairloch dan lima bangsawan Mackenzie gagal memburunya; menurut Patricia Monaghan tak pernah terlihat lagi.",
        "en": "Sir Hector Mackenzie of Gairloch and five Mackenzie dignitaries failed to catch him; per Patricia Monaghan never seen again."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ghillie-dhu-c06",
      "source_id": "ghillie-dhu-s2",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel eu: nama ghillie suit (ghillie jantzia) terkait tokoh ini.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gigelorum (Giol-Daoram) adalah serangga dalam folklor Skotlandia yang dipercaya sebagai makhluk terkecil, hidup di telinga tungau.",
    "en": "The Gigelorum (Giol-Daoram) is an insect of Scottish folklore held to be the smallest creature, living in a mite's ear.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gigelorum-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gigelorum (atau Giol-Daoram) adalah serangga dalam folklor Skotlandia yang dipercaya sebagai makhluk paling kecil. Tidak ada deskripsi atau keterangan lain tentangnya selain bahwa ia hidup di telinga tungau. Ada dugaan ia didasarkan pada serangga terkecil yang dapat dilihat orang berpenglihatan sangat tajam, giolcam-daobhram, yang digambarkan sebagai \"makhluk hidup terkecil yang dapat dibayangkan\".",
      "en": "The Gigelorum (or Giol-Daoram) is an insect of Scottish folklore thought to be the tiniest of all creatures. Nothing is recorded of it beyond its home inside a mite's ear. It may be modelled on the smallest insect visible to someone with excellent eyesight, the giolcam-daobhram, described as the least living thing one could suppose.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gigelorum-c01",
        "gigelorum-c02",
        "gigelorum-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ronald Black, mantan dosen studi Kelt, penulis dan jurnalis, berpendapat makhluk ini mungkin hanya rekaan folkloris dan pendeta Tiree John Gregorson Campbell, karena ia tidak menemukan sumber yang berwenang tentangnya.",
      "en": "Ronald Black, a former Celtic studies lecturer, author and journalist, thinks the creature may have been invented by the folklorist and Tiree minister John Gregorson Campbell, since he found no authoritative source for it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gigelorum-c03",
        "gigelorum-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

      "id": "gigelorum-c01",
      "source_id": "gigelorum-s1",
      "quote": "Gigelorum (or Giol-Daoram) is an insect of Scottish folklore. It was believed to be the smallest creature. No description or information is available about it except that it inhabits the ear of a mite.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gigelorum (Giol-Daoram), serangga folklor Skotlandia (Scottish), makhluk terkecil, hidup di telinga tungau.",
        "en": "Gigelorum (Giol-Daoram), an insect of Scottish folklore, smallest creature, lives in a mite's ear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gigelorum-c02",
      "source_id": "gigelorum-s1",
      "quote": "Speculation exists that it may be based upon the tiniest insect that can be discerned by people possessing excellent eyesight, the giolcam-daobhram",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mungkin berdasar serangga terkecil yang terlihat, giolcam-daobhram.",
        "en": "Possibly based on the tiniest visible insect, the giolcam-daobhram."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gigelorum-c03",
      "source_id": "gigelorum-s1",
      "quote": "Ronald Black, a one time Celtic studies lecturer, author and journalist, suggests that the creature could be a figment of the imagination of folklorist and Tiree minister John Gregorson Campbell",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

      }
    },
    {

      "source_id": "gigelorum-s2",
      "quote": "Si tratta di un insetto minuscolo, ritenuto essere il più piccolo animale esistente. Le sue caratteristiche sono ignote, eccezion fatta per il suo habitat, le orecchie degli acari.",

      "statement": {

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Glas Gaibhnenn adalah sapi ajaib pembawa kelimpahan dalam folklor Irlandia yang menghasilkan susu sangat banyak.",
    "en": "Glas Gaibhnenn is a prized magical cow of plenty in Irish folklore that gives vast amounts of milk.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "glas-gaibhnenn-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Irlandia, Glas Gaibhnenn (juga Glas Ghaibhleann, Glas Gaivlen) adalah sapi ajaib kesuburan yang sangat berharga dan menghasilkan susu berlimpah. Pemiliknya bervariasi: seorang pandai besi yang mungkin bernama Gaivnin, atau pahlawan Cian mac Cáinte, yang setara dengan Cian ayah Lugh. Sapi itu dicuri oleh Balar atau Balor; untuk merebutnya kembali, sang pahlawan dibawa seorang banshee ke menara tempat putri Balor dikurung, sehingga lahir anak yang ditakdirkan membunuh Balor.",
      "en": "In Irish folklore Glas Gaibhnenn (also Glas Ghaibhleann, Glas Gaivlen) is a precious fabulous cow of fertility that gives milk in great abundance. Its owner varies: a smith perhaps named Gaivnin, or the hero Cian mac Cáinte, matching Cian father of Lugh. Balar or Balor steals the cow; in the quest to recover it the hero is carried by a banshee to the tower where Balor's daughter is kept, and a child is born who is fated to kill Balor.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "glas-gaibhnenn-c01",
        "glas-gaibhnenn-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Komentator setuju bahwa glas (biru, hijau, kelabu) adalah warna bulunya tetapi berbeda pendapat tentang rinciannya: Larminie mengartikan nama lengkapnya \"Sapi Kelabu milik Pandai Besi\", sedangkan Rhys \"Sapi Kelabu milik Goibniu\". Artikel ga mencatat Patricia Monaghan menyebutnya dewi yang sering dikaitkan dengan sungai, dan Patrick Power mendaftarnya di antara makhluk mitos yang namanya dipakai untuk bentang alam.",
      "en": "Commentators agree that glas, 'blue, green, grey', names the colour of its coat but differ on detail: Larminie reads the full name as 'Grey (cow) of the Smith', Rhys as 'Goibniu's Grey (Cow)'. The ga article notes Patricia Monaghan calling it a goddess often linked with rivers, and Patrick Power listing it among mythical beasts after which landscape features were named.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "glas-gaibhnenn-c04",
        "glas-gaibhnenn-c03"
      ]
    }
  ],
